    <itunes:summary><![CDATA[Cloud Security Podcast by Google focuses on security in the cloud, delivering security from the cloud, and all things at the intersection of security and cloud. Of course, we will also cover what we are doing in Google Cloud to help keep our users' data safe and workloads secure. 

We're going to do our best to avoid security theater, and cut to the heart of real security questions and issues. Expect us to question threat models and ask if something is done for the data subject's benefit or just for organizational benefit. 

We hope you'll join us if you're interested in where technology overlaps with process and bumps up against organizational design. We're hoping to attract listeners who are happy to hear conventional wisdom questioned, and who are curious about what lessons we can and can't keep as the world moves from on-premises computing to cloud computing.]]></itunes:summary>

    <description><![CDATA[Cloud Security Podcast by Google focuses on security in the cloud, delivering security from the cloud, and all things at the intersection of security and cloud. Of course, we will also cover what we are doing in Google Cloud to help keep our users' data safe and workloads secure. 

We're going to do our best to avoid security theater, and cut to the heart of real security questions and issues. Expect us to question threat models and ask if something is done for the data subject's benefit or just for organizational benefit. 

We hope you'll join us if you're interested in where technology overlaps with process and bumps up against organizational design. We're hoping to attract listeners who are happy to hear conventional wisdom questioned, and who are curious about what lessons we can and can't keep as the world moves from on-premises computing to cloud computing.]]></description>

    <item>
      <title>EP292 Inside Chrome Security: AI Patching, Agents, Rust, and … Your Tabs</title>
      <itunes:title>EP292 Inside Chrome Security: AI Patching, Agents, Rust, and … Your Tabs</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Tue, 25 Aug 2026 14:01:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[83fbee60-6c4a-4769-991c-d430aa276e64]]></guid>
      <link><![CDATA[https://cloudsecuritypodcast.libsyn.com/ep292-inside-chrome-security-ai-patching-agents-rust-and-your-tabs]]></link>
      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guests:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Jasika Bawa, Group Product Manager, Chrome Security</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Doug Turner, Engineering Director, Chrome Security</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Gemini agent harness found a sandbox escape that survived in the codebase for 13 years! How did that happen and how did that bug get noticed among what's surely a tidal wave of bugs being found by the machines?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Chrome 149 and 150 fixed over a thousand security bugs—surpassing the last 23 milestones combined. That is a massive spike! T<a href= "https://blog.google/security/chrome-stronger-with-every-update/">he blog</a> explains it's possible by having 'fixing agents' and 'critic agents' working in a loop that mimics human code reviews. I have to ask: how much of this is real, high-quality engineering, and how much of it is just two robots agreeing to grade each other on a curve?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Historically, pushing updates was a monumental, scheduled event. Now, you're piloting two security releases a week to combat fast-moving attacks. That sounds like a direct route to developer burnout, and a complete nightmare for enterprise IT admins who just want their systems to stay stable. How are we balancing the need to protect users from N-day exploits versus making everyone play security whack-a-mole?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">I want to learn more about this long-term vision so let's address the elephant in the browser: nobody wants to restart Chrome. Personally, I have about 50 tabs open right now, and restarting feels like risking throwing my short-term memory into a woodchipper. You're talking about 'dynamic patching' that replaces background child processes on the fly without a restart, and you've rolled out a 'zero window auto-restart' on macOS. First off, are my tabs safe? Second, is dynamic patching actually going to work seamlessly, or is it just going to crash my Renderer mid-session and blame it on the GPU? If this is real, it's really amazing. </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Tell us about the "spannification" effort and the migration to Rust. There's two parallel technical safety efforts going on to–as we like to say at Google–eliminate classes of vulnerabilities. Tell us about the engineering efforts behind the C++ spans and the Rust journey! </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Ok I'd be a terrible Allan Friedman disciple if I didn't ask the supply chain question. First, Chrome has over 2,300 third-party dependencies. That's wild. How are we handling that?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://youtu.be/pCXT8lQqg_U">Video version</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://blog.google/security/chrome-stronger-with-every-update/">"Stronger with every update: How we're making Chrome and the web safer in the AI Era"</a> blog</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/next-special-improving-browser-security-in-the-new-era-of-work/"> EP88 Improving Browser Security in the New Era of Work</a></li> </ul>]]></description>
      
      <content:encoded><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guests:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Jasika Bawa, Group Product Manager, Chrome Security</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Doug Turner, Engineering Director, Chrome Security</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Gemini agent harness found a sandbox escape that survived in the codebase for 13 years! How did that happen and how did that bug get noticed among what's surely a tidal wave of bugs being found by the machines?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Chrome 149 and 150 fixed over a thousand security bugs—surpassing the last 23 milestones combined. That is a massive spike! T<a href= "https://blog.google/security/chrome-stronger-with-every-update/">he blog</a> explains it's possible by having 'fixing agents' and 'critic agents' working in a loop that mimics human code reviews. I have to ask: how much of this is real, high-quality engineering, and how much of it is just two robots agreeing to grade each other on a curve?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Historically, pushing updates was a monumental, scheduled event. Now, you're piloting two security releases a week to combat fast-moving attacks. That sounds like a direct route to developer burnout, and a complete nightmare for enterprise IT admins who just want their systems to stay stable. How are we balancing the need to protect users from N-day exploits versus making everyone play security whack-a-mole?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">I want to learn more about this long-term vision so let's address the elephant in the browser: nobody wants to restart Chrome. Personally, I have about 50 tabs open right now, and restarting feels like risking throwing my short-term memory into a woodchipper. You're talking about 'dynamic patching' that replaces background child processes on the fly without a restart, and you've rolled out a 'zero window auto-restart' on macOS. First off, are my tabs safe? Second, is dynamic patching actually going to work seamlessly, or is it just going to crash my Renderer mid-session and blame it on the GPU? If this is real, it's really amazing. </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Tell us about the "spannification" effort and the migration to Rust. There's two parallel technical safety efforts going on to–as we like to say at Google–eliminate classes of vulnerabilities. Tell us about the engineering efforts behind the C++ spans and the Rust journey! </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Ok I'd be a terrible Allan Friedman disciple if I didn't ask the supply chain question. First, Chrome has over 2,300 third-party dependencies. That's wild. How are we handling that?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://youtu.be/pCXT8lQqg_U">Video version</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://blog.google/security/chrome-stronger-with-every-update/">"Stronger with every update: How we're making Chrome and the web safer in the AI Era"</a> blog</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/next-special-improving-browser-security-in-the-new-era-of-work/"> EP88 Improving Browser Security in the New Era of Work</a></li> </ul>]]></content:encoded>

      <title>EP291  Ruthless Prioritization: How CISOs Can Execute a Minimum Viable Security Program</title>
      <itunes:title>EP291  Ruthless Prioritization: How CISOs Can Execute a Minimum Viable Security Program</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Tue, 18 Aug 2026 00:47:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[34756105-ebcf-4cd4-8fa7-6d06572e12a8]]></guid>
      <link><![CDATA[https://cloudsecuritypodcast.libsyn.com/ep291-ruthless-prioritization-how-cisos-can-execute-a-minimum-viable-security-program]]></link>
      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/mike-armistead-1164715/">Mike Armistead</a>,  CEO, Pulse Security AI</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/danlamorena/">Dan Lamorena</a>, Chief Go-To-Market Officer,  <a href= "https://pulsesecurity.ai/">Pulse Security AI</a></li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics: </p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You've described Pulse as an 'operational management platform for cybersecurity leaders.' What does a security management platform look like in practice? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">How does Pulse bridge this 'translation layer' gap? How do you help a CISO transition from presenting patch rates and MTTD to discussing liability exposure and business capital allocation?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What actually gets better when someone works with Pulse? Other than, of course, your ARR numbers for your next funding round?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What is the single most surprising or alarming disconnect you've identified in your CISO and Board Engagement Survey? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You've introduced the concept of the 'Minimum Viable Security Program (MV(S)P)' and emphasized that 'focus is power.' In an era of non-stop vendor noise, compliance updates, and emerging AI threats, how does Pulse help a CISO ruthlessly prioritize and execute their MV(S)P without getting distracted by the noise?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Your company is officially 'Pulse Security AI,' so AI is central to your brand. To put on our healthy skeptic hats: how does Pulse pragmatically leverage AI to solve the CISO's actual day-to-day program management problems, rather than just adding to the marketing noise?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://youtu.be/FlzRtYwXbZg">Video</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep114-minimal-viable-secure-product-mvsp-is-that-a-thing/"> EP114 Minimal Viable Secure Product (MVSP) - Is That a Thing?</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep208-the-modern-ciso-balancing-risk-innovation-and-business-strategy-and-where-is-cloud/"> EP208 The Modern CISO: Balancing Risk, Innovation, and Business Strategy (And Where is Cloud?)</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep201-every-cto-should-be-a-csto-or-else-transformation-lessons-from-the-hoff/"> EP201 Every CTO Should Be a CSTO (Or Else!) - Transformation Lessons from The Hoff</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep204-beyond-pcast-phil-venables-on-the-future-of-resilience-and-leading-indicators/"> EP204 Beyond PCAST: Phil Venables on the Future of Resilience and Leading Indicators</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.amazon.com/dp/0316296619?lv=shuf&channelId=500&plpRedirect=mhFallback"> "The Bomber Mafia: A Dream, a Temptation, and the Longest Night of the Second World War"</a> by Malcolm Gladwell </li> </ul>]]></description>
      
      <content:encoded><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/mike-armistead-1164715/">Mike Armistead</a>, CEO, Pulse Security AI</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/danlamorena/">Dan Lamorena</a>, Chief Go-To-Market Officer, <a href= "https://pulsesecurity.ai/">Pulse Security AI</a></li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics: </p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You've described Pulse as an 'operational management platform for cybersecurity leaders.' What does a security management platform look like in practice? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">How does Pulse bridge this 'translation layer' gap? How do you help a CISO transition from presenting patch rates and MTTD to discussing liability exposure and business capital allocation?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What actually gets better when someone works with Pulse? Other than, of course, your ARR numbers for your next funding round?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What is the single most surprising or alarming disconnect you've identified in your CISO and Board Engagement Survey? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You've introduced the concept of the 'Minimum Viable Security Program (MV(S)P)' and emphasized that 'focus is power.' In an era of non-stop vendor noise, compliance updates, and emerging AI threats, how does Pulse help a CISO ruthlessly prioritize and execute their MV(S)P without getting distracted by the noise?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Your company is officially 'Pulse Security AI,' so AI is central to your brand. To put on our healthy skeptic hats: how does Pulse pragmatically leverage AI to solve the CISO's actual day-to-day program management problems, rather than just adding to the marketing noise?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://youtu.be/FlzRtYwXbZg">Video</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep114-minimal-viable-secure-product-mvsp-is-that-a-thing/"> EP114 Minimal Viable Secure Product (MVSP) - Is That a Thing?</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep208-the-modern-ciso-balancing-risk-innovation-and-business-strategy-and-where-is-cloud/"> EP208 The Modern CISO: Balancing Risk, Innovation, and Business Strategy (And Where is Cloud?)</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep201-every-cto-should-be-a-csto-or-else-transformation-lessons-from-the-hoff/"> EP201 Every CTO Should Be a CSTO (Or Else!) - Transformation Lessons from The Hoff</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep204-beyond-pcast-phil-venables-on-the-future-of-resilience-and-leading-indicators/"> EP204 Beyond PCAST: Phil Venables on the Future of Resilience and Leading Indicators</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.amazon.com/dp/0316296619?lv=shuf&channelId=500&plpRedirect=mhFallback"> "The Bomber Mafia: A Dream, a Temptation, and the Longest Night of the Second World War"</a> by Malcolm Gladwell </li> </ul>]]></content:encoded>

      <title>EP290 Project Atlas: Wiz's AI Vulnerability Research</title>
      <itunes:title>EP290 Project Atlas: Wiz&#39;s AI Vulnerability Research</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Mon, 10 Aug 2026 14:15:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[9b96281d-8beb-458c-9fe6-51b80deceb7e]]></guid>

      <description><![CDATA[<p>ZeroDay Cloud: How a hacking competition exposed the reality of AI-augmented offensive security.</p> <p>The Power of Multi-Agent AI: Why a single model is not enough, and how specialized agents (Threat Modeling, Hunting, Triage, and Adversarial Debate) collaborate like a human team.</p> <p>GitHub RCE & CosmosDB Cross Tenant Access: A breakdown of the record-breaking bounty for the GitHub RCE report and the Azure CosmosDB master key vulnerability discovered by Atlas.</p> <p>Humans vs. AI: Whether vulnerability researchers are becoming "buggy whip manufacturers" or if human insight, instinct, and the smell of danger remain irreplaceable.</p> <p> </p> <p>https://www.wiz.io/blog/atlas-ai-vulnerability-researcher</p>]]></description>
      
      <content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>ZeroDay Cloud: How a hacking competition exposed the reality of AI-augmented offensive security.</p> <p>The Power of Multi-Agent AI: Why a single model is not enough, and how specialized agents (Threat Modeling, Hunting, Triage, and Adversarial Debate) collaborate like a human team.</p> <p>GitHub RCE & CosmosDB Cross Tenant Access: A breakdown of the record-breaking bounty for the GitHub RCE report and the Azure CosmosDB master key vulnerability discovered by Atlas.</p> <p>Humans vs. AI: Whether vulnerability researchers are becoming "buggy whip manufacturers" or if human insight, instinct, and the smell of danger remain irreplaceable.</p> <p> </p> <p>https://www.wiz.io/blog/atlas-ai-vulnerability-researcher</p>]]></content:encoded>

    <item>
      <title>EP289 Software Engineering vs. Software Craft: How Google Scalably Eliminates Classes of Vulnerabilities</title>
      <itunes:title>EP289 Software Engineering vs. Software Craft: How Google Scalably Eliminates Classes of Vulnerabilities</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Mon, 03 Aug 2026 12:00:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[0fcf1d4e-d676-4ce0-b987-fb1b4a04a31f]]></guid>
      <link><![CDATA[https://cloudsecuritypodcast.libsyn.com/ep289-software-engineering-vs-software-craft-how-google-scalably-eliminates-classes-of-vulnerabilities]]></link>
      <description><![CDATA[<p><span data-markdown-start-index="141">How do you build the foundations for a secure Google-scale enterprise that stays secure</span> <em><span data-markdown-start-index="196">even if</span></em> <span data-markdown-start-index="204">an AI is writing the code and nobody has time to review it? In this episode, hosts Timothy Peacock and Anton Chuvakin sit down with Christoph Kern, Principal Security Engineer at Google, to look under the hood of "secure-by-design." They trace Google's 15-year engineering journey to fundamentally eliminate entire classes of vulnerabilities rather than just playing whack-a-mole with bugs after they are written.</span></p> <p><strong><span data-markdown-start-index="617">🛠️ Software Engineering vs. Software Craft:</span></strong><span data-markdown-start-index= "667"> Why Google ditched "be careful" programming checklists in favor of hard compiler and framework-level invariants that make security defects physically impossible to build.</span></p> <p><strong><span data-markdown-start-index="837">📦 Hiding the Risky Abstractions:</span></strong><span data-markdown-start-index= "874"> How replacing high-risk programming constructs (like raw pointers in C++ or raw injection sinks in browser code) with safe, compiler-enforced abstractions secures codebases at a scale humans can no longer manually audit.</span></p> <p><strong><span data-markdown-start-index="1096">📢 "Marketing Didn't Write It":</span></strong><span data-markdown-start-index= "1131"> Christoph and the hosts unpack why the concept of "eliminating a class of vulnerabilities" is a rigorous, mathematical reality at Google rather than just public relations hype.</span></p> <p><strong><span data-markdown-start-index="1309">🤝 Empathy vs. Opinionated Platforms:</span></strong><span data-markdown-start-index= "1350"> The secret feedback loop Google uses to build highly opinionated developer platforms (like Boq or browser-native safe types) that protect systems without turning developers into frustrated "software artists" fighting the compiler.</span></p> <p><strong><span data-markdown-start-index="1582">📈 The Android Productivity Proof:</span></strong><span data-markdown-start-index= "1620"> The real-world metrics from Google's Android team proving that shifting to memory-safe languages (like Rust) dramatically drops vulnerabilities while actually </span><em><span data-markdown-start-index= "1781">boosting</span></em><span data-markdown-start-index= "1790"> developer velocity and lowering rollback rates</span></p>]]></description>
      
      <content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>How do you build the foundations for a secure Google-scale enterprise that stays secure <em>even if</em> an AI is writing the code and nobody has time to review it? In this episode, hosts Timothy Peacock and Anton Chuvakin sit down with Christoph Kern, Principal Security Engineer at Google, to look under the hood of "secure-by-design." They trace Google's 15-year engineering journey to fundamentally eliminate entire classes of vulnerabilities rather than just playing whack-a-mole with bugs after they are written.</p> <p>🛠️ Software Engineering vs. Software Craft: Why Google ditched "be careful" programming checklists in favor of hard compiler and framework-level invariants that make security defects physically impossible to build.</p> <p>📦 Hiding the Risky Abstractions: How replacing high-risk programming constructs (like raw pointers in C++ or raw injection sinks in browser code) with safe, compiler-enforced abstractions secures codebases at a scale humans can no longer manually audit.</p> <p>📢 "Marketing Didn't Write It": Christoph and the hosts unpack why the concept of "eliminating a class of vulnerabilities" is a rigorous, mathematical reality at Google rather than just public relations hype.</p> <p>🤝 Empathy vs. Opinionated Platforms: The secret feedback loop Google uses to build highly opinionated developer platforms (like Boq or browser-native safe types) that protect systems without turning developers into frustrated "software artists" fighting the compiler.</p> <p>📈 The Android Productivity Proof: The real-world metrics from Google's Android team proving that shifting to memory-safe languages (like Rust) dramatically drops vulnerabilities while actually <em>boosting</em> developer velocity and lowering rollback rates</p>]]></content:encoded>

      <content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>Can you build a fully functional, high-scale SIEM in just two weeks for under $7,000?</p> <p>In this episode of the <em>Cloud Security Podcast</em>, hosts Tim Peacock and Kyle Champlin sit down with long-time collaborator Dan Lucier, Founder of Nano, to unpack how he "vibe-coded" an entire SIEM from scratch during his end-of-year holiday break.</p> <p>Dan shares his journey of leveraging bleeding-edge AI code assistants to go from a Postgres prototype to a blazing-fast, production-ready SIEM built on Rust and ClickHouse.</p> <p>In this episode, we cover:</p> <ul> <li> <p>🛠️ The $7,000 Stack: How Dan utilized Claude Code and Kubernetes to build a lean platform running on 2 vCPUs and 4GB RAM while ingesting 10–20 GB of data daily.</p> </li> <li> <p>⚡ Why ClickHouse?       <title>EP279 Native Cloud Security: Is 'Good Enough' Actually Winning?</title>
      <itunes:title>EP279 Native Cloud Security: Is &#39;Good Enough&#39; Actually Winning?</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Mon, 25 May 2026 14:11:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[80a55bf9-dc26-4b2f-bfd3-a402e779e590]]></guid>
      <link><![CDATA[https://cloudsecuritypodcast.libsyn.com/ep279-native-cloud-security-is-good-enough-actually-winning]]></link>
      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guests:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/galordo/">Gal Ordo</a>, Co-founder & CPO @ <a href="https://native.security/">Native</a></li> </ul> <p><strong> </strong>Topics:<strong> </strong></p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep186-cloud-security-tools-trust-the-cloud-provider-or-go-third-party-an-epic-debate-anton-vs-tim/"> In Episode 186</a>, we debated 'Native vs. Third-Party' as a binary choice. Native seems to be a third-party vendor whose entire existence depends on the belief that cloud-native controls are superior. Does your platform validate the 'Cloud Provider' side of the debate (that their controls are enough), or does the fact that you exist prove the 'Third-Party' side (that native interfaces aren't enough)?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">A key argument against native controls is an AWS WAF and a Google Cloud Armor don't behave the same way. If your tool manages native controls across multi-cloud, how do you handle the 'lowest common denominator' problem? Do you dumb down the policy to fit all clouds, or do you expose the unique complexity of each one?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">GuardDuty and SCC produce similar but meaningfully different results. How do you abstract across that so an analyst or IR team isn't having to dig into the exact meaning of the different JSON fields in their output?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">We often say native tools are 'good enough' for 80% of use cases but lack the depth of specialized third-party vendors (like a dedicated CNAPP or DLP). By betting your company on orchestrating native controls, are you effectively betting that 'good enough' is the future of the market? What happens when a customer needs a feature that the CSP hasn't built yet?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What fraction of your users are taking this from a "I'm 80% this one cloud, I need great coverage there and good enough elsewhere" vs "I'm truly multi-cloud" or even scarier "I have a workload that is active spanning clouds"? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Do your customers push you towards helping with the kinds of SaaS platforms that SSPM vendors cover?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">If AWS and Google Cloud suddenly decided to make their native security UIs perfect and unified tomorrow, would your company cease to exist? Or is the complexity of the cloud strictly increasing, guaranteeing you job security forever?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Related:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://youtu.be/QMXFmNjA6B0">Video version</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep186-cloud-security-tools-trust-the-cloud-provider-or-go-third-party-an-epic-debate-anton-vs-tim/"> EP186 Cloud Security Tools: Trust the Cloud Provider or Go Third-Party? An Epic Debate, Anton vs Tim</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep160-dont-cloud-your-judgement-security-and-cloud-migration-again/"> EP160 Don't Cloud Your Judgement: Security and Cloud Migration, Again!</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://medium.com/anton-on-security/the-great-cloud-security-debate-csp-vs-third-party-security-tools-6563e3dc6ac9"> The Great Cloud Security Debate: CSP vs. Third-Party Security Tools</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://native.security/blog">native.security blog</a></li> </ul>]]></description>
      
      <content:encoded><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guests:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/galordo/">Gal Ordo</a>, Co-founder & CPO @ <a href="https://native.security/">Native</a></li> </ul> <p> Topics: </p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep186-cloud-security-tools-trust-the-cloud-provider-or-go-third-party-an-epic-debate-anton-vs-tim/"> In Episode 186</a>, we debated 'Native vs. Third-Party' as a binary choice. Native seems to be a third-party vendor whose entire existence depends on the belief that cloud-native controls are superior. Does your platform validate the 'Cloud Provider' side of the debate (that their controls are enough), or does the fact that you exist prove the 'Third-Party' side (that native interfaces aren't enough)?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">A key argument against native controls is an AWS WAF and a Google Cloud Armor don't behave the same way. If your tool manages native controls across multi-cloud, how do you handle the 'lowest common denominator' problem? Do you dumb down the policy to fit all clouds, or do you expose the unique complexity of each one?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">GuardDuty and SCC produce similar but meaningfully different results. How do you abstract across that so an analyst or IR team isn't having to dig into the exact meaning of the different JSON fields in their output?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">We often say native tools are 'good enough' for 80% of use cases but lack the depth of specialized third-party vendors (like a dedicated CNAPP or DLP). By betting your company on orchestrating native controls, are you effectively betting that 'good enough' is the future of the market? What happens when a customer needs a feature that the CSP hasn't built yet?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What fraction of your users are taking this from a "I'm 80% this one cloud, I need great coverage there and good enough elsewhere" vs "I'm truly multi-cloud" or even scarier "I have a workload that is active spanning clouds"? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Do your customers push you towards helping with the kinds of SaaS platforms that SSPM vendors cover?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">If AWS and Google Cloud suddenly decided to make their native security UIs perfect and unified tomorrow, would your company cease to exist? Or is the complexity of the cloud strictly increasing, guaranteeing you job security forever?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Related:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://youtu.be/QMXFmNjA6B0">Video version</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep186-cloud-security-tools-trust-the-cloud-provider-or-go-third-party-an-epic-debate-anton-vs-tim/"> EP186 Cloud Security Tools: Trust the Cloud Provider or Go Third-Party? An Epic Debate, Anton vs Tim</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep160-dont-cloud-your-judgement-security-and-cloud-migration-again/"> EP160 Don't Cloud Your Judgement: Security and Cloud Migration, Again!</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://medium.com/anton-on-security/the-great-cloud-security-debate-csp-vs-third-party-security-tools-6563e3dc6ac9"> The Great Cloud Security Debate: CSP vs. Third-Party Security Tools</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://native.security/blog">native.security blog</a></li> </ul>]]></content:encoded>

      <title>EP278 The Agentic SOC: Are We Measuring Time Saved or Risk Reduced?</title>
      <itunes:title>EP278 The Agentic SOC: Are We Measuring Time Saved or Risk Reduced?</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Mon, 18 May 2026 13:16:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[6349d024-d2f9-4d19-86c9-4a662ddc83dd]]></guid>
      <link><![CDATA[https://cloudsecuritypodcast.libsyn.com/ep278-the-agentic-soc-are-we-measuring-time-saved-or-risk-reduced]]></link>
      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/mattgregsonpwc/">Matt Gregson</a>, Principal - PwC Cyber Security</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What is the state of the art of "agentic SOC" in 2026? Can you describe the most agentic SOC you've seen so far?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">In your experience, what are the main measurable benefits of AI agents in a SOC and IR?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Imagine a 2030 SOC, what do humans do?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Tell us more about how you judge if a client SOC is ready for AI and agents? What is the "Ouch" moment where most organizations realize their data isn't ready for that level of autonomy?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Should we be more afraid of "AI hallucinations" or "Human fatigue" in the SOC? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">If a team has an agentic teammate making its own decisions based on emergent reasoning, how do you audit its "thought process"?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Everyone loves to talk about "Time Saved," but in an agentic SOC, we care about "Decision Quality." What is the one metric PwC uses to prove that a SOC agent  deployment is actually reducing risk?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">We often hear about "human-agent teaming." Are they still looking at alerts, or are they just approving "Action Plans" generated by the AI?<strong><br /></strong></li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://youtu.be/gER5oFS9Bpw">Video version</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep236-accelerated-siem-journey-a-soc-leaders-playbook-for-modernization-and-ai/"> EP236 Accelerated SIEM Journey: A SOC Leader's Playbook for Modernization and AI</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep252-the-agentic-soc-reality-governing-ai-agents-data-fidelity-and-measuring-success/"> EP252 The Agentic SOC Reality: Governing AI Agents, Data Fidelity, and Measuring Success</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep264-measuring-your-agentic-soc-two-security-leaders-walk-into-a-podcast/"> EP264 Measuring Your (Agentic) SOC: Two Security Leaders Walk into a Podcast</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/topics/podcast-list/?tag=siem-soc"> All SOC and SIEM episodes</a></li> </ul>]]></description>

      <title>EP277: CISO as CFO, From Citi to Celery, It's All about the Cabbage</title>
      <itunes:title>EP277: CISO as CFO, From Citi to Celery, It&#39;s All about the Cabbage</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Wed, 13 May 2026 16:12:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[69ae5338-b652-44d5-b21a-b818340ad5f8]]></guid>
      <link><![CDATA[https://cloudsecuritypodcast.libsyn.com/ep277-ciso-as-cfo-from-citi-to-celery-its-all-about-the-cabbage]]></link>
      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/arvinbansal/">Arvin Bansal</a>, CISO, C&S Wholesale Grocers</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Most people do not associate grocery wholesale and retail with cutting edge technology and threat models. Can you produce the receipts for why this isn't a story of dry goods but rather a very meaty topic with beefy adversaries? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">How are you as the CISO enabling C&S's journey into AI and LLM driven work? Securing AI is a bit harder than securing classic analytics tools, right?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">In addition to securely rolling out AI, how is your defense team using AI to secure C&S? Are you into the era of agentic triage and response?  What metrics for AI is your D&R lead surfacing up to you?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You have AI in the business process that - if failed - will leave people hungry. How do you approach AI resilience? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">How do you approach resilience in general? Is cloud part of your resilience strategy?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You worked at Citigroup for a long time. What's it like having grocery margin budgets for security instead? How does your thinking change? Does this shift your build/buy/outsource for security? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">If your IoT stack falls over, you've got literal ice cream melting in a warehouse. How do you balance your investments in cyber risk with physical operational risk? Should I be scared of forklifts?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep275-google-cloud-next-2026-the-ai-earthquake-soc-home-syndrome-and-the-ragged-edge-of-reality/"> EP275 Google Cloud Next 2026: The AI Earthquake, "SOC-home" Syndrome, and the Ragged Edge of Reality</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep247-the-evolving-ciso-from-security-cop-to-cloud-ai-champion/"> EP247 The Evolving CISO: From Security Cop to Cloud & AI Champion</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep208-the-modern-ciso-balancing-risk-innovation-and-business-strategy-and-where-is-cloud/"> EP208 The Modern CISO: Balancing Risk, Innovation, and Business Strategy (And Where is Cloud?)</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep212-securing-the-cloud-at-scale-modern-bank-ciso-on-metrics-challenges-and-secops/"> EP212 Securing the Cloud at Scale: Modern Bank CISO on Metrics, Challenges, and SecOps</a></li> </ul>]]></description>

      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Episode co-host:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "mailto:mkaganovich@google.com"><span data-rich-links= "{"per_n":"Marina Kaganovich","per_e":"mkaganovich@google.com","type":"person"}"> Marina Kaganovich</span></a>, Enterprise Trust Lead, Office of the CISO, Google Cloud</li> </ul> <p><strong> </strong>Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.bakerlaw.com/professionals/james-a-sherer/">James Sherer, Partner at BakerHostetler</a></li> </ul> <p><strong> </strong>Topics</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Is AI just an emerging technology or something bigger, deeper and different?  Is this another emerging technology or a fundamental shift?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">How to effectively govern something that is rapidly changing at unprecedented velocity?  We navigated the governance of the Internet and SaaS. What makes AI governance fundamentally different from the "Classic IT" or Data Governance models of the past?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">As we move toward Agentic AI, the line between tool and teammate blurs. Where are the tipping points as we shift from assistance to autonomy?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">How to avoid unintended, negative consequences when setting policy, contrasting risk-based vs. rights-based regulation and regulatory expectations</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Give us some practical takeaways for a defensible AI program - if an organization had to defend its AI program to a regulator or a judge tomorrow?<strong> </strong></li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Related episodes:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://youtu.be/mxS9-Zl2pHA">Video version</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep235-the-autonomous-frontier-governing-ai-agents-from-code-to-courtroom/"> EP235 The Autonomous Frontier: Governing AI Agents from Code to Courtroom</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep161-cloud-compliance-a-lawyer-turned-technologist-perspective-on-navigating-the-cloud/"> EP161 Cloud Compliance: A Lawyer - Turned Technologist! - Perspective on Navigating the Cloud</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep237-making-security-personal-at-the-speed-and-scale-of-tiktok/"> EP237 Making Security Personal at the Speed and Scale of TikTok</a></li> </ul>]]></description>

      <title>EP274 AI, Zero Trust and Secure by Design Walk into a Bar...</title>
      <itunes:title>EP274 AI, Zero Trust and Secure by Design Walk into a Bar...</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Mon, 27 Apr 2026 14:11:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[cfb47259-d4b7-403e-8e4a-b11352e745de]]></guid>
      <link><![CDATA[https://cloudsecuritypodcast.libsyn.com/ep274-ai-zero-trust-and-secure-by-design-walk-into-a-bar]]></link>
      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/grant-dasher/">Grant Dasher,</a> ex-CISA, ex-Google, Distinguished Engineer, Google (again)</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Why is the  "Secure-by-Design" movement gaining so much momentum now, and is it a response to the failure of "bolted-on" security, or just a natural evolution of cloud maturity?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">In a future Secure-by-Design world, is identity the only perimeter that actually matters anymore? Or is this a cliche?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">As we move toward a world of autonomous agents, how does our approach to machine identity need to change? Are we just talking about more complex Service Accounts, or do we need a fundamental shift in how we authorize "intent"</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What is your  advice  to people who want to move fast and cannot wait for Secure by Design / Default  AI to be decided by consensus or IETF, NIST or OASIS committee?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">We love the argument that modern AI agents are effectively repeating the mistakes of 1960s payphones - mixing the data plane and the control plane. What is your rebuttal? How do we build "Agentic Security" that doesn't fall for 60-year-old traps?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Customers are torn between their Zero Trust implementations and their AI adoption. Is Zero Trust now "legacy," or is it the prerequisite for everything we're trying to do with AI agents?  </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Is there Zero Trust for AI? Is this a fake buzzword or technical reality?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://youtu.be/B7e1UYoszWg">Video version</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep256-rewiring-democracy-hacking-trust-bruce-schneier-on-the-ai-offense-defense-balance/"> EP256 Rewiring Democracy & Hacking Trust: Bruce Schneier on the AI Offense-Defense Balance</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep133-the-shared-problem-of-alerting-more-sre-lessons-for-security/"> EP133 The Shared Problem of Alerting: More SRE Lessons for Security</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep85-deploy-security-capabilities-at-scale-sre-explains-how/"> EP85 Deploy Security Capabilities at Scale: SRE Explains How</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://sre.google/books/">Google SRE books</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.amazon.com/Atomic-Accidents-Meltdowns-Disasters-Mountains/dp/1605986801"> "Atomic Accidents"</a> book (yes, really)</li> </ul>]]></description>
      
      <content:encoded><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/grant-dasher/">Grant Dasher,</a> ex-CISA, ex-Google, Distinguished Engineer, Google (again)</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Why is the "Secure-by-Design" movement gaining so much momentum now, and is it a response to the failure of "bolted-on" security, or just a natural evolution of cloud maturity?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">In a future Secure-by-Design world, is identity the only perimeter that actually matters anymore? Or is this a cliche?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">As we move toward a world of autonomous agents, how does our approach to machine identity need to change? Are we just talking about more complex Service Accounts, or do we need a fundamental shift in how we authorize "intent"</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What is your advice to people who want to move fast and cannot wait for Secure by Design / Default AI to be decided by consensus or IETF, NIST or OASIS committee?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">We love the argument that modern AI agents are effectively repeating the mistakes of 1960s payphones - mixing the data plane and the control plane. What is your rebuttal? How do we build "Agentic Security" that doesn't fall for 60-year-old traps?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Customers are torn between their Zero Trust implementations and their AI adoption. Is Zero Trust now "legacy," or is it the prerequisite for everything we're trying to do with AI agents? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Is there Zero Trust for AI? Is this a fake buzzword or technical reality?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://youtu.be/B7e1UYoszWg">Video version</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep256-rewiring-democracy-hacking-trust-bruce-schneier-on-the-ai-offense-defense-balance/"> EP256 Rewiring Democracy & Hacking Trust: Bruce Schneier on the AI Offense-Defense Balance</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep133-the-shared-problem-of-alerting-more-sre-lessons-for-security/"> EP133 The Shared Problem of Alerting: More SRE Lessons for Security</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep85-deploy-security-capabilities-at-scale-sre-explains-how/"> EP85 Deploy Security Capabilities at Scale: SRE Explains How</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://sre.google/books/">Google SRE books</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.amazon.com/Atomic-Accidents-Meltdowns-Disasters-Mountains/dp/1605986801"> "Atomic Accidents"</a> book (yes, really)</li> </ul>]]></content:encoded>

      <title>EP273 From CISA to Cloud: AI Assurance, Concentration Risk, and the New Regulatory Frontier</title>
      <itunes:title>EP273 From CISA to Cloud: AI Assurance, Concentration Risk, and the New Regulatory Frontier</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Mon, 20 Apr 2026 14:11:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[b70a621b-a8d9-4bfd-ae8d-2070fb2aee9f]]></guid>

      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/jeanette-manfra-297681a/">Jeanette Manfra</a>, VP, Head of Risk and Compliance, Google Cloud</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">How does "outsourcing" security to the cloud change the intensity of the security vs. privacy struggle for a CISO?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Does the centralization of cloud make it a bigger target for regulators, or is there a dimension we're missing?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Does the Shared Responsibility Model actually survive contact with regulators, and how does AI complicate that boundary?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Can AI actually automate the translation of fragmented rules into evidence, or are we just dreaming?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">How do we navigate the collision between transparency (logging everything) and privacy (recording nothing)?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What is your one piece of practical advice for leaders helping their teams adopt AI?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://youtu.be/T4BezLex3xI">Video version</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/making-compliance-cloud-native/"> EP14 Making Compliance Cloud-native</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep161-cloud-compliance-a-lawyer-turned-technologist-perspective-on-navigating-the-cloud/"> EP161 Cloud Compliance: A Lawyer - Turned Technologist! - Perspective on Navigating the Cloud</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep258-why-your-security-strategy-needs-an-immune-system-not-a-fortress-with-royal-hansen/"> EP258 Why Your Security Strategy Needs an Immune System, Not a Fortress with Royal Hansen</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep126-what-is-policy-as-code-and-how-can-it-help-you-secure-your-cloud-environment/"> EP126 What is Policy as Code and How Can It Help You Secure Your Cloud Environment?</a></li> </ul>]]></description>
      
      <content:encoded><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/jeanette-manfra-297681a/">Jeanette Manfra</a>, VP, Head of Risk and Compliance, Google Cloud</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">How does "outsourcing" security to the cloud change the intensity of the security vs. privacy struggle for a CISO?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Does the centralization of cloud make it a bigger target for regulators, or is there a dimension we're missing?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Does the Shared Responsibility Model actually survive contact with regulators, and how does AI complicate that boundary?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Can AI actually automate the translation of fragmented rules into evidence, or are we just dreaming?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">How do we navigate the collision between transparency (logging everything) and privacy (recording nothing)?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What is your one piece of practical advice for leaders helping their teams adopt AI?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://youtu.be/T4BezLex3xI">Video version</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/making-compliance-cloud-native/"> EP14 Making Compliance Cloud-native</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep161-cloud-compliance-a-lawyer-turned-technologist-perspective-on-navigating-the-cloud/"> EP161 Cloud Compliance: A Lawyer - Turned Technologist!       <title>EP272 More Than Just Packets: Is NDR a "First-Class" Cloud Security Control?</title>
      <itunes:title>EP272 More Than Just Packets: Is NDR a &quot;First-Class&quot; Cloud Security Control?</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Mon, 13 Apr 2026 14:11:00 +0000</pubDate>

      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/rajamukerji/">Raja Mukerji</a>, Co-Founder & Chief Scientist, Extrahop</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/rmlos/">Rafal Los</a>, VP of Client Relations and Strategic Initiatives, Extrahop</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Is Network Detection and Response (NDR) coming back after being shoved to the side by EDR a bit?       <title>EP271 Can AI-Native MDR Actually Fix Your Broken SOC Workflows or Just Automate the Mess?</title>
      <itunes:title>EP271 Can AI-Native MDR Actually Fix Your Broken SOC Workflows or Just Automate the Mess?</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Thu, 09 Apr 2026 17:04:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[74e05516-a338-427f-a3a7-70033a8426b7]]></guid>
      <link><![CDATA[https://cloudsecuritypodcast.libsyn.com/ep271-can-ai-native-mdr-actually-fix-your-broken-soc-workflows-or-just-automate-the-mess]]></link>
      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guests:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/ericfoster/">Eric Foster</a>, CEO, <a href="http://tenex.ai">Tenex.AI</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/bashar-abouseido-279581/">Bashar Abouseido</a>, President,  <a href= "http://tenex.ai">Tenex.AI</a></li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">"10X SOC" sounds great.  But for an organization stuck in "SIEM 1.0" with poor data quality and manual workflows, is "AI-native MDR" a "leapfrog" opportunity or a recipe for disaster?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">We've seen the rise of <a href= "https://medium.com/anton-on-security/decoupled-siem-where-i-think-we-are-now-89ab9f3df43f"> "Decoupled SIEM"</a> and security data lakes. Does a "Modern SIEM" even need to exist if an MDR platform has an agentic layer doing the heavy lifting? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You've argued for AI-native over AI-bolted-on. For an end user, what are the tangible differences of using "AI inside a legacy SIEM" versus using an "AI-native separate product"?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What is the one task you thought AI would handle by now that still requires a senior human analyst to step in?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">If a CISO is using an AI MDR, "Mean Time to Detect" (MTTD) starts to look like a <a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep252-the-agentic-soc-reality-governing-ai-agents-data-fidelity-and-measuring-success/"> vanity metric</a> because the machine is instant. What is the new golden metric for an AI-powered SOC? Is it "Time to Context," "Reduction in Human Toil," or something else?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">How do you help a skeptical SOC Manager—who has been burned by false positives for a decade—trust an autonomous agent to perform a "containment" action at 3:00 AM? <strong> </strong></li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep227-ai-native-mdr-betting-on-the-future-of-security-operations/"> EP227 AI-Native MDR: Betting on the Future of Security Operations?</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/siem-modernization-is-that-a-thing/"> EP10 SIEM Modernization? Is That a Thing?</a></li> <li>The original "10X" paper <a href= "https://services.google.com/fh/files/misc/white_paper_autonomic_security_operations.pdf"> "Autonomic Security Operations: 10X Transformation of the Security Operations Center"</a></li> </ul>]]></description>
      
      <content:encoded><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guests:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/ericfoster/">Eric Foster</a>, CEO, <a href="http://tenex.ai">Tenex.AI</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/bashar-abouseido-279581/">Bashar Abouseido</a>, President, <a href= "http://tenex.ai">Tenex.AI</a></li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">"10X SOC" sounds great.       <title>EP270 The Convenience Tax: Why We Keep Failing at Supply Chain Security</title>
      <itunes:title>EP270 The Convenience Tax: Why We Keep Failing at Supply Chain Security</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Mon, 06 Apr 2026 14:11:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[abacdbcf-3848-41a0-a408-36947c339a03]]></guid>
      <link><![CDATA[https://cloudsecuritypodcast.libsyn.com/ep270-the-convenience-tax-why-we-keep-failing-at-supply-chain-security]]></link>
      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/danlorenc/">Dan Lorenc</a>, Founder / CEO, <a href="https://www.chainguard.dev/">Chainguard</a></li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">We just saw a security tool (Trivy) get used to pop an AI infrastructure tool (LiteLLM) to eventually pop end users. Have we reached the point where our security tooling is actually our largest unmanaged attack surface? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Why now? Software supply chain security had the perennial vibe of "not top concern" for most organizations, right?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">TeamPCP pushed malicious code to existing GitHub tags. We've been screaming about pinning versions to SHAs for years, but clearly, nobody is listening. Is it time to admit that 'convenience' is the primary enemy of supply chain security?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">The Axios incident showed a victim compromised in under two minutes. In a world of auto-updating dependencies, is the concept of a human-in-the-loop for software updates officially dead, or do we need to look very hard at version pinning and such?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">With XZ Utils case, we saw a long-game social engineering attack. Beyond just 'watching npm closely,' what are the realistic architectural safeguards for an org that knows they can't audit every line of an update?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">We've spent the last three years talking about SBOMs (Software Bill of Materials) like they were a pill for supply chain health. But if the scanner producing the SBOM is the one that's compromised, isn't the SBOM just a signed receipt for your own house being on fire? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What is the one practical thing they can do to ensure their CI/CD isn't a credential-exfiltration-as-a-service platform?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://youtu.be/zOhq4OEmd_o">Video version</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.google.com/blog/topics/threat-intelligence/north-korea-threat-actor-targets-axios-npm-package"> North Korea-Nexus Threat Actor Compromises Widely Used Axios NPM Package in Supply Chain Attack</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep100-2022-accelerate-state-of-devops-report-and-software-supply-chain-security/"> EP100 2022 Accelerate State of DevOps Report and Software Supply Chain Security</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep116-sboms-a-step-towards-a-more-secure-software-supply-chain/"> EP116 SBOMs: A Step Towards a More Secure Software Supply Chain</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep226-ai-supply-chain-security-old-lessons-new-poisons-and-agentic-dreams/"> EP226 AI Supply Chain Security: Old Lessons, New Poisons, and Agentic Dreams</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/linking-up-the-pieces-software-supply-chain-security-at-google-and-beyond/"> EP24 Linking Up The Pieces: Software Supply Chain Security at Google and Beyond</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://mattlevine.co/work">Matt Levine blog</a></li> </ul>]]></description>
      
      <content:encoded><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/danlorenc/">Dan Lorenc</a>, Founder / CEO, <a href="https://www.chainguard.dev/">Chainguard</a></li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">We just saw a security tool (Trivy) get used to pop an AI infrastructure tool (LiteLLM) to eventually pop end users. Have we reached the point where our security tooling is actually our largest unmanaged attack surface? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Why now? Software supply chain security had the perennial vibe of "not top concern" for most organizations, right?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">TeamPCP pushed malicious code to existing GitHub tags. We've been screaming about pinning versions to SHAs for years, but clearly, nobody is listening. Is it time to admit that 'convenience' is the primary enemy of supply chain security?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">The Axios incident showed a victim compromised in under two minutes. In a world of auto-updating dependencies, is the concept of a human-in-the-loop for software updates officially dead, or do we need to look very hard at version pinning and such?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">With XZ Utils case, we saw a long-game social engineering attack. Beyond just 'watching npm closely,' what are the realistic architectural safeguards for an org that knows they can't audit every line of an update?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">We've spent the last three years talking about SBOMs (Software Bill of Materials) like they were a pill for supply chain health. But if the scanner producing the SBOM is the one that's compromised, isn't the SBOM just a signed receipt for your own house being on fire? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What is the one practical thing they can do to ensure their CI/CD isn't a credential-exfiltration-as-a-service platform?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://youtu.be/zOhq4OEmd_o">Video version</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.google.com/blog/topics/threat-intelligence/north-korea-threat-actor-targets-axios-npm-package"> North Korea-Nexus Threat Actor Compromises Widely Used Axios NPM Package in Supply Chain Attack</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep100-2022-accelerate-state-of-devops-report-and-software-supply-chain-security/"> EP100 2022 Accelerate State of DevOps Report and Software Supply Chain Security</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep116-sboms-a-step-towards-a-more-secure-software-supply-chain/"> EP116 SBOMs: A Step Towards a More Secure Software Supply Chain</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep226-ai-supply-chain-security-old-lessons-new-poisons-and-agentic-dreams/"> EP226 AI Supply Chain Security: Old Lessons, New Poisons, and Agentic Dreams</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/linking-up-the-pieces-software-supply-chain-security-at-google-and-beyond/"> EP24 Linking Up The Pieces: Software Supply Chain Security at Google and Beyond</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://mattlevine.co/work">Matt Levine blog</a></li> </ul>]]></content:encoded>

      <title>EP267 AI SOC or AI in a SOC? Cutting Through Hype, Pricing Models, and SIEM Detection Efficacy with Raffy Marty</title>
      <itunes:title>EP267 AI SOC or AI in a SOC? Cutting Through Hype, Pricing Models, and SIEM Detection Efficacy with Raffy Marty</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Mon, 16 Mar 2026 14:11:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[0ea32e4c-379c-48b8-9867-57b00ef71fa4]]></guid>
      <link><![CDATA[https://cloudsecuritypodcast.libsyn.com/ep267-ai-soc-or-ai-in-a-soc-cutting-through-hype-pricing-models-and-siem-detection-efficacy-with-raffy-marty]]></link>
      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/raffy/">Raffael Marty</a>, Operating Advisor, a SIEM legend since 1999</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You argue that declaring existing SIEM being obsolete is a "marketing slogan" rather than a true thesis. What is the real pain point and the actual gap in traditional SIEMs as opposed to the more sensational claims?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You highlight that "correlation, state, timelines, and real-time detection require locality," making centralization a necessary trade-off. Can a truly federated or decoupled SIEM architecture achieve the same fidelity and real-time performance for complex, stateful detections as a centralized one?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You call the rise of independent security data pipelines the "SIEM Trojan Horse." How quickly is this abstraction layer turning SIEM into a "swappable" component, and what should SIEM vendors have done differently years ago to prevent this market from existing?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">This "AI SOC" thing, is this even real? Is AI in a SOC a better label? Do you think major SIEM vendors will own this very soon, like they did with UEBA and SOAR?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">If volume-based pricing is flawed because it penalizes good security hygiene, what is a better SIEM pricing model that fairly addresses compute, enrichment, and retention costs without just shifting the volume cost to unpredictable query charges?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You question the idea that startups can find a better way to release detection rules than large vendors with significant content teams. What metrics should security leaders use to evaluate the quality of a vendor's detection engineering (DE) output beyond just coverage numbers? Can AI fix DE?</li> </ul> <p> </p> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://youtu.be/ndXX7WbMCXE">Video version</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://raffy.ch/blog/2026/02/11/the-siem-maturity-framework-workbook-v1-0-a-practical-scoring-tool-for-security-analytics-platforms/"> The SIEM Maturity Framework: A Practical Scoring Tool for Security Analytics Platforms</a> and <a href= "https://raffy.ch/SIEM/">raffy.ch/SIEM/</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://raffy.ch/blog/2026/02/03/the-gaps-that-created-the-new-wave-of-siem-and-ai-soc-vendors/"> The Gaps That Created the New Wave of SIEM and AI SOC Vendors</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://raffy.ch/blog/2026/01/16/how-ai-impacts-the-cyber-market-and-the-future-of-siem/"> How AI Impacts the Cyber Market and The Future of SIEM</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://raffy.ch/blog/2025/12/17/why-venture-capital-is-betting-against-traditional-siems/"> Why Venture Capital Is Betting Against Traditional SIEMs</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep236-accelerated-siem-journey-a-soc-leaders-playbook-for-modernization-and-ai/"> EP236 Accelerated SIEM Journey: A SOC Leader's Playbook for Modernization and AI</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep125-will-siem-ever-die-siem-lessons-from-the-past-for-the-future/"> EP125 Will SIEM Ever Die: SIEM Lessons from the Past for the Future</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://medium.com/anton-on-security/decoupled-siem-brilliant-or-stupid-ada2c3142b2b"> Decoupled SIEM: Brilliant or Stupid?</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://medium.com/anton-on-security/decoupled-siem-where-i-think-we-are-now-89ab9f3df43f"> Decoupled SIEM: Where I Think We Are Now?</a></li> </ul>]]></description>
      
      <content:encoded><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/raffy/">Raffael Marty</a>, Operating Advisor, a SIEM legend since 1999</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You argue that declaring existing SIEM being obsolete is a "marketing slogan" rather than a true thesis. What is the real pain point and the actual gap in traditional SIEMs as opposed to the more sensational claims?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You highlight that "correlation, state, timelines, and real-time detection require locality," making centralization a necessary trade-off. Can a truly federated or decoupled SIEM architecture achieve the same fidelity and real-time performance for complex, stateful detections as a centralized one?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You call the rise of independent security data pipelines the "SIEM Trojan Horse." How quickly is this abstraction layer turning SIEM into a "swappable" component, and what should SIEM vendors have done differently years ago to prevent this market from existing?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">This "AI SOC" thing, is this even real? Is AI in a SOC a better label? Do you think major SIEM vendors will own this very soon, like they did with UEBA and SOAR?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">If volume-based pricing is flawed because it penalizes good security hygiene, what is a better SIEM pricing model that fairly addresses compute, enrichment, and retention costs without just shifting the volume cost to unpredictable query charges?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You question the idea that startups can find a better way to release detection rules than large vendors with significant content teams. What metrics should security leaders use to evaluate the quality of a vendor's detection engineering (DE) output beyond just coverage numbers? Can AI fix DE?</li> </ul> <p> </p> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://youtu.be/ndXX7WbMCXE">Video version</a></li> <li dir="ltr" Practical Scoring Tool for Security Analytics Platforms</a> and <a href= "https://raffy.ch/SIEM/">raffy.ch/SIEM/</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href=       <title>EP266 Resetting the SOC for Code War: Allie Mellen on Detecting State Actors vs. Doing the Basics</title>
      <itunes:title>EP266 Resetting the SOC for Code War: Allie Mellen on Detecting State Actors vs. Doing the Basics</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Mon, 09 Mar 2026 15:11:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[ab8daa86-4d6a-4416-815d-3be7ad32fd3b]]></guid>
      <link><![CDATA[https://cloudsecuritypodcast.libsyn.com/ep266-resetting-the-soc-for-code-war-allie-mellen-on-detecting-state-actors-vs-doing-the-basics]]></link>
      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/hackerxbella/">Allie Mellen</a>, <a href= "https://www.forrester.com/analyst-bio/allie-mellen/BIO16084">Principal Analyst @ Forrester</a>, author of <a href= "https://www.amazon.com/Code-War-Nations-Digital-Battlefield/dp/1394285574"> "Code War: How Nations Hack, Spy, and Shape the Digital Battlefield"</a></li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Your book focuses on the US, China, and Russia. When you were planning the book did you also want to cover players like Israel, Iran, and North Korea?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Most of our listeners are migrating to or operating heavily in the cloud. As nations refine their "digital battlefield" strategies, does the "shared responsibility model" actually hold up against a nation-state actor?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">How does a company's detection strategy need to change when the adversary isn't a teenager looking for a ransom, but a state-funded group whose goal might be long-term persistence or subtle data manipulation? How should people allocate their resources to defending against both of these threats? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">How afraid are you of a "bad guy with AI" scenarios? Mild anxiety or apocalyptic fears? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Do you see AI primarily helping "Tier 2" nations close the capability gap with the "Big Three," or does it just further cement the dominance of the nations that own the underlying compute and models?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You've spent a lot of time as an analyst looking at how enterprises buy and run security tech. For a CISO at (say) mid-tier logistics company, should 'nation-state cyberattacks' even be on their threat model? Or is worrying about the spies just a form of security theater when they haven't even solved basic credential theft yet?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resource:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://youtu.be/HfUmmNA93PQ">Video version</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.amazon.com/Code-War-Nations-Digital-Battlefield/dp/1394285574"> "Code War: How Nations Hack, Spy, and Shape the Digital Battlefield"</a> by Allie Mellen</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://substack.com/@hackerxbella">Allie Mellen substack</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://taosecurity.blogspot.com/2008/12/justifying-national-security-spending.html"> The source for the original "air defense on the roof"</a> argument (2008)</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep255-separating-hype-from-hazard-the-truth-about-autonomous-ai-hacking/"> EP255 Separating Hype from Hazard: The Truth About Autonomous AI Hacking</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep256-rewiring-democracy-hacking-trust-bruce-schneier-on-the-ai-offense-defense-balance/"> EP256 Rewiring Democracy & Hacking Trust: Bruce Schneier on the AI Offense-Defense Balance</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep156-living-off-the-land-and-attacking-critical-infrastructure-mandiant-incident-deep-dive/"> EP156 Living Off the Land and Attacking Critical Infrastructure: Mandiant Incident Deep Dive</a></li> <li><a href= "https://www.anthropic.com/news/disrupting-AI-espionage">"Disrupting the first reported AI-orchestrated cyber espionage campaign"</a> report</li> </ul>]]></description>

      <title>EP265 Beyond Shadow IT: Unsanctioned AI Agents Don't Just Talk, They Act!</title>
      <itunes:title>EP265 Beyond Shadow IT: Unsanctioned AI Agents Don&#39;t Just Talk, They Act!</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Mon, 02 Mar 2026 14:48:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[90560a53-cba3-4270-b3c8-9ec63e7b9ba8]]></guid>
      <link><![CDATA[https://cloudsecuritypodcast.libsyn.com/ep265-beyond-shadow-it-unsanctioned-ai-agents-dont-just-talk-they-act]]></link>
      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/alastair-paterson-2586445/">Alastair Paterson</a>, CEO and co-founder @ <a href= "https://www.harmonic.security/">Harmonic Security</a></li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.harmonic.security/">Harmonic Security</a> focuses on securing generative AI in use. Can you walk us through a real, anonymized example of a data leak caused by employee AI usage that your platform has identified?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">AI governance gets thrown around a lot. What does this mean in the context of Shadow AI? How should organizations be thinking about governing AI in light of upcoming AI regulations in the US and in the EU?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">If we generally agree that employees are using AI tools before they are sanctioned, how can organizations control this?
