      <title>EP264  Measuring Your (Agentic) SOC: Two Security Leaders Walk into a Podcast</title>
      <itunes:title>EP264  Measuring Your (Agentic) SOC: Two Security Leaders Walk into a Podcast</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Mon, 23 Feb 2026 15:02:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[fc4943d2-856b-4257-b7a7-3e744c4675a7]]></guid>
      <link><![CDATA[https://cloudsecuritypodcast.libsyn.com/ep264-measuring-your-agentic-soc-two-security-leaders-walk-into-a-podcast]]></link>
      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guests:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/apabst/">Alexander  Pabst</a>, Global Deputy CISO, Allianz SE</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/michael-sinno-a83b60/">Michael Sinno</a>, Director of D&R, Google</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">We've spent decades obsessed with MTTD (Mean Time to Detect) and MTTR (Mean Time to Respond). As AI agents begin to handle the bulk of triage at machine speed, do these metrics become "vanity metrics"? If an AI resolves an alert in seconds, does measuring the "mean" still tell us anything about the health of our security program, or should we be looking at "Time to Context" instead?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You mentioned the Maturity Triangle. Can you walk us through that framework? Specifically, how does AI change the balance between the three points of that triangle—is it shifting us from a "People-heavy" model to something more "Engineering-led," and where does the "Measurement" piece sit?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Google is famous for its <a href= "https://cloud.google.com/blog/products/identity-security/modernizing-soc-introducing-autonomic-security-operations"> "Engineering-led" approach to D&R</a>. How is Google currently measuring the success of its own internal D&R program? Specifically, how are you quantifying "Toil Reduction"? Are we measuring how many hours we saved, or are we measuring the complexity of the threats our humans are now free to hunt?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Toil reduction is a laudable goal for the team members, what are the metrics we track and report up to document the overall improvement in D&R for Google's board?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">When you talk to your board about the success of AI in your security program, what are the 2 or 3 "Golden Metrics" that actually move the needle for them? How do you prove that an AI-driven SOC is actually better, not just faster?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">We often talk about AI as an "assistant," but we're moving toward Agentic SOCs. How should organizations measure the "unit economics" of their SOC? Should we be tracking the ratio of AI-handled vs. Human-handled incidents, and at what point does a high AI-handle rate become a risk rather than a success?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://youtu.be/ZNyvd__9vuk">Video version</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep252-the-agentic-soc-reality-governing-ai-agents-data-fidelity-and-measuring-success/"> EP252 The Agentic SOC Reality: Governing AI Agents, Data Fidelity, and Measuring Success</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep238-google-lessons-for-using-ai-agents-for-securing-our-enterprise/"> EP238 Google Lessons for Using AI Agents for Securing Our Enterprise</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep91-hacking-google-op-aurora-and-insider-threat-at-google/"> EP91 "Hacking Google", Op Aurora and Insider Threat at Google</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep236-accelerated-siem-journey-a-soc-leaders-playbook-for-modernization-and-ai/"> EP236 Accelerated SIEM Journey: A SOC Leader's Playbook for Modernization and AI</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep189-how-google-does-security-programs-at-scale-ciso-insights/"> EP189 How Google Does Security Programs at Scale: CISO Insights</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep75-how-we-scale-detection-and-response-at-google-automation-metrics-toil/"> EP75 How We Scale Detection and Response at Google: Automation, Metrics, Toil</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://security.googlecloudcommunity.com/ciso-blog-77/the-soc-metrics-that-matter-or-do-they-3946"> The SOC Metrics that Matter…or Do They?</a> blog</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.forrester.com/blogs/an-actual-complete-list-of-soc-metrics-and-your-path-to-diy/"> An Actual Complete List Of SOC Metrics (And Your Path To DIY)</a> blog</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.google.com/blog/products/identity-security/mind-your-metrics-to-achieve-better-autonomic-security-operations"> Achieving Autonomic Security Operations: Why metrics matter (but not how       <content:encoded><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guests:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/apabst/">Alexander Pabst</a>, Global Deputy CISO, Allianz SE</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/michael-sinno-a83b60/">Michael Sinno</a>, Director of D&R, Google</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">We've spent decades obsessed with MTTD (Mean Time to Detect) and MTTR (Mean Time to Respond). As AI agents begin to handle the bulk of triage at machine speed, do these metrics become "vanity metrics"? If an AI resolves an alert in seconds, does measuring the "mean" still tell us anything about the health of our security program, or should we be looking at "Time to Context" instead?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You mentioned the Maturity Triangle. Can you walk us through that framework? Specifically, how does AI change the balance between the three points of that triangle—is it shifting us from a "People-heavy" model to something more "Engineering-led," and where does the "Measurement" piece sit?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Google is famous for its <a href= "https://cloud.google.com/blog/products/identity-security/modernizing-soc-introducing-autonomic-security-operations"> "Engineering-led" approach to D&R</a>. How is Google currently measuring the success of its own internal D&R program? Specifically, how are you quantifying "Toil Reduction"? Are we measuring how many hours we saved, or are we measuring the complexity of the threats our humans are now free to hunt?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Toil reduction is a laudable goal for the team members, what are the metrics we track and report up to document the overall improvement in D&R for Google's board?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">When you talk to your board about the success of AI in your security program, what are the 2 or 3 "Golden Metrics" that actually move the needle for them? How do you prove that an AI-driven SOC is actually better, not just faster?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">We often talk about AI as an "assistant," but we're moving toward Agentic SOCs. How should organizations measure the "unit economics" of their SOC? Should we be tracking the ratio of AI-handled vs. Human-handled incidents, and at what point does a high AI-handle rate become a risk rather than a success?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://youtu.be/ZNyvd__9vuk">Video version</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= Fidelity, and Measuring Success</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= Enterprise</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep91-hacking-google-op-aurora-and-insider-threat-at-google/"> EP91 "Hacking Google",       <title>EP262 Freedom, Responsibility, and the Federated Guardrails: A New Model for Modern Security</title>
      <itunes:title>EP262 Freedom, Responsibility, and the Federated Guardrails: A New Model for Modern Security</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Mon, 09 Feb 2026 15:11:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[e0796351-62d1-49b9-81c3-d7dc7bee3d62]]></guid>
      <link><![CDATA[https://cloudsecuritypodcast.libsyn.com/ep262-freedom-responsibility-and-the-federated-guardrails-a-new-model-for-modern-security]]></link>
      <description><![CDATA[<p>Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/shulmana/">Alex Shulman-Peleg</a>, Global CISO at <a href="https://kraken.tech">Kraken</a></li> </ul> <p><strong> </strong>Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You mentioned that centralized security <em>can't</em> work anymore. Can you elaborate on the key changes—driven by cloud, SaaS, and AI—that have made this traditional model unsustainable for a modern organization?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Why do some persist at centralized, top down approach to security, despite that?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What do you mean by "Freedom, Responsibility and distributed security"? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Can you explain the difference between "centralized security" and what you define as "security with distributed ownership"?  Is this the same "federated"?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">In our conversation you mentioned "cloud and AI- native", what do you mean by this (especially "AI-native") and how is this changing your approach to security? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You introduce the concept of "Security as quality" suggesting that a security-unaware developer is essentially a bad software developer. How do you shift the culture and internal metrics to make security an inherent quality standard, rather than a separate, compliance-driven checklist?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You likened the central security team's new role to a "911 emergency service." Beyond incident response, what stays central no matter what, and how does the central team successfully influence the security posture of the entire organization without being directly responsible for the day-to-day work.</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://youtu.be/obAuLU3EnUM">Video version</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep129-how-ciso-cloud-dreams-and-realities-collide/"> EP129 How CISO Cloud Dreams and Realities Collide</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep258-why-your-security-strategy-needs-an-immune-system-not-a-fortress-with-royal-hansen/"> EP258 Why Your Security Strategy Needs an Immune System, Not a Fortress with Royal Hansen</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep212-securing-the-cloud-at-scale-modern-bank-ciso-on-metrics-challenges-and-secops/"> EP212 Securing the Cloud at Scale: Modern Bank CISO on Metrics, Challenges, and SecOps</a></li> </ul>]]></description>

      <content:encoded><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" aria-level="1"> <p dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/dwchow/">Dennis Chow</a>, Director of Detection Engineering at UKG</p> </li> </ul> <p> Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">We ended our season <a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep255-separating-hype-from-hazard-the-truth-about-autonomous-ai-hacking/"> talking about the AI apocalypse</a>. In your opinion, are we living in the world that the guests describe in <a href= "https://www.csoonline.com/article/4069075/autonomous-ai-hacking-and-the-future-of-cybersecurity.html"> their apocalypse paper</a>? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Do you think AI-powered attacks are really here, and if so, what is your plan to respond? Is it faster patching? Better D&R? Something else altogether? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Your team has a hybrid agent workflow: could you tell us what that means? Also, define "AI agent" please.</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What are your production use cases for AI and AI agents in your SOC?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What are your overall SOC metrics and how does the agentic AI part play into that?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">It's one thing to ask a team "hey what did y'all do last week" and get a good report - how are you measuring the agentic parts of your SOC?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">How are you thinking about what comes next once AI is automatically writing good (!) rules for your team out of research blog posts and TI papers? </li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://youtu.be/KJmZ6tWDMV0">Video version</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/agentic-ai-soc-build-vs-buy-lessons-mustapha-kebbeh-msgye/"> Agentic AI in the SOC: Build vs Buy Lessons</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep255-separating-hype-from-hazard-the-truth-about-autonomous-ai-hacking/"> EP255 Separating Hype from Hazard: The Truth About Autonomous AI Hacking</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep256-rewiring-democracy-hacking-trust-bruce-schneier-on-the-ai-offense-defense-balance/"> EP256 Rewiring Democracy & Hacking Trust: Bruce Schneier on the AI Offense-Defense Balance</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep252-the-agentic-soc-reality-governing-ai-agents-data-fidelity-and-measuring-success/"> EP252 The Agentic SOC Reality: Governing AI Agents, Data Fidelity, and Measuring Success</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep236-accelerated-siem-journey-a-soc-leaders-playbook-for-modernization-and-ai/"> EP236 Accelerated SIEM Journey: A SOC Leader's Playbook for Modernization and AI</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep242-the-ai-soc-is-this-the-automation-weve-been-waiting-for/"> EP242 The AI SOC: Is This The Automation We've Been Waiting For?</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.google.com/resources/boost-your-cloud-skills-with-google"> Google Cloud Skill Boost</a></li> </ul> <p> </p>]]></content:encoded>

      <title>EP260 The Agentic IAM Trainwreck: Why Your Bots Need Better Permissions Than Your Admins</title>

      <pubDate>Mon, 26 Jan 2026 15:11:00 +0000</pubDate>

      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/vishwasmanral/">Vishwas Manral</a>, CEO at <a href="https://www.precize.ai/">Precize.ai</a></li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topic:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Why is agent security so different from "just" LLM security?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Why now?       <title>EP258 Why Your Security Strategy Needs an Immune System, Not a Fortress with Royal Hansen</title>
      <itunes:title>EP258 Why Your Security Strategy Needs an Immune System, Not a Fortress with Royal Hansen</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Mon, 12 Jan 2026 15:11:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[570203d1-7dd7-40e9-a2d1-4d604355b53c]]></guid>
      <link><![CDATA[https://cloudsecuritypodcast.libsyn.com/ep258-why-your-security-strategy-needs-an-immune-system-not-a-fortress-with-royal-hansen]]></link>
      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/royal-hansen-989858/">Royal Hansen</a>, VP of Engineering at Google, former CISO of Alphabet</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">The "God-Like Designer" Fallacy: You've argued that we need to move away from the "God-like designer" model of security—where we pre-calculate every risk like building a bridge—and towards a biological model. Can you explain why that old engineering mindset is becoming risky in today's cloud and AI environments?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Resilience vs. Robustness: In your view, what is the practical difference between a robust system (like a fortress that eventually breaks) and a resilient system (like an immune system)? How does a CISO start shifting their team's focus from creating the former to nurturing the latter?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Securing the Unknown: We're entering an era where <a href= "https://security.googlecloudcommunity.com/ciso-blog-77/10-tips-for-governing-ai-agents-6081"> AI agents</a> will call other agents, creating pathways we never explicitly designed. If we can't predict these interactions, how can we possibly secure them? What does "emergent security" look like in practice?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Primitives for Agents: You mentioned the need for new "biological primitives" for these agents—things like time-bound access or inherent throttling. Are these just new names for old concepts like Zero Trust, or is there something different about how we need to apply them to AI?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">The Compliance Friction: There's a massive tension between this dynamic, probabilistic reality and the static, checklist-based world of many compliance regimes. How do you, as a leader, bridge that gap? How do you convince an auditor or a board that a "probabilistic" approach doesn't just mean "we don't know for sure"?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"> "Safe" Failures: How can organizations get comfortable with the idea of designing for allowable failure in their subsystems, rather than striving for 100% uptime and security everywhere?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://youtu.be/1RfrEfBrjYg">Video version</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep189-how-google-does-security-programs-at-scale-ciso-insights/"> EP189 How Google Does Security Programs at Scale: CISO Insights</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.google.com/blog/products/identity-security/cloud-ciso-perspectives-our-big-sleep-agent-makes-big-leap"> BigSleep</a> and <a href= "https://deepmind.google/blog/introducing-codemender-an-ai-agent-for-code-security/"> CodeMender</a> agents</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.amazon.com/Chasing-Rabbit-Outdistance-Competition-Christensen/dp/0071499881"> "Chasing the Rabbit"</a> book  </li> <li><a href= "https://www.amazon.com/dp/0226826686?ref_=ppx_hzsearch_conn_dt_b_fed_asin_title_27"> "How Life Works: A User's Guide to the New Biology"</a> book</li> </ul>]]></description>
      
      <content:encoded><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/royal-hansen-989858/">Royal Hansen</a>, VP of Engineering at Google, former CISO of Alphabet</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">The "God-Like Designer" Fallacy: You've argued that we need to move away from the "God-like designer" model of security—where we pre-calculate every risk like building a bridge—and towards a biological model. Can you explain why that old engineering mindset is becoming risky in today's cloud and AI environments?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Resilience vs. Robustness: In your view, what is the practical difference between a robust system (like a fortress that eventually breaks) and a resilient system (like an immune system)? How does a CISO start shifting their team's focus from creating the former to nurturing the latter?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Securing the Unknown: We're entering an era where <a href= "https://security.googlecloudcommunity.com/ciso-blog-77/10-tips-for-governing-ai-agents-6081"> AI agents</a> will call other agents, creating pathways we never explicitly designed. If we can't predict these interactions, how can we possibly secure them? What does "emergent security" look like in practice?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Primitives for Agents: You mentioned the need for new "biological primitives" for these agents—things like time-bound access or inherent throttling. Are these just new names for old concepts like Zero Trust, or is there something different about how we need to apply them to AI?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">The Compliance Friction: There's a massive tension between this dynamic, probabilistic reality and the static, checklist-based world of many compliance regimes. How do you, as a leader, bridge that gap? How do you convince an auditor or a board that a "probabilistic" approach doesn't just mean "we don't know for sure"?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"> "Safe" Failures: How can organizations get comfortable with the idea of designing for allowable failure in their subsystems, rather than striving for 100% uptime and security everywhere?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://youtu.be/1RfrEfBrjYg">Video version</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep189-how-google-does-security-programs-at-scale-ciso-insights/"> EP189 How Google Does Security Programs at Scale: CISO Insights</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.google.com/blog/products/identity-security/cloud-ciso-perspectives-our-big-sleep-agent-makes-big-leap"> BigSleep</a> and <a href= "https://deepmind.google/blog/introducing-codemender-an-ai-agent-for-code-security/"> CodeMender</a> agents</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.amazon.com/Chasing-Rabbit-Outdistance-Competition-Christensen/dp/0071499881"> "Chasing the Rabbit"</a> book </li> <li><a href= "https://www.amazon.com/dp/0226826686?ref_=ppx_hzsearch_conn_dt_b_fed_asin_title_27"> "How Life Works: A User's Guide to the New Biology"</a> book</li> </ul>]]></content:encoded>

      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[30cf0747-c07d-4d52-add0-6f5985fb5661]]></guid>

      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li><a href="https://www.linkedin.com/in/chrissistrunk/">Chris Sistrunk</a>, Technical Leader, OT Consulting, Mandiant</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">When we hear "attacks on Operational Technology (OT)" some think of Stuxnet targeting PLCs or even backdoored pipeline control software plot in the 1980s. Is this space always so spectacular or are there less "kaboom" style attacks we are more concerned about in practice?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Given the old "air-gapped" mindset of many OT environments, what are the most common security gaps or blind spots you see when organizations start to integrate cloud services for things like data analytics or remote monitoring?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">How is the shift to cloud connectivity - for things like data analytics, centralized management, and remote access -  changing the security posture of these systems? What's a real-world example of a positive security outcome you've seen as a direct result of this cloud adoption?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">How do the Tactics, Techniques, and Procedures outlined in the <a href= "https://attack.mitre.org/matrices/ics/">MITRE ATT&CK for ICS</a> framework change or evolve when attackers can leverage cloud-based reconnaissance and command-and-control infrastructure to target OT networks? Can you provide an example?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">OT environments are generating vast amounts of operational data. What is interesting for OT Detection and Response (D&R)?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://youtu.be/mrqF62DgOFc">Video version</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.google.com/security/resources/cybersecurity-forecast"> Cybersecurity Forecast 2026 report</a> by Google</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.google.com/transform/complex-hybrid-manufacturing-needs-strong-security-how-cisos-get-it-done"> Complex, hybrid manufacturing needs strong security. Here's how CISOs can get it done</a> blog</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://services.google.com/fh/files/misc/ot_security_blueprint_ociso2025.pdf"> "Security Guidance for Cloud-Enabled Hybrid Operational Technology Networks"</a> paper by <a href="https://gcat.google.com">Google Cloud Office of the CISO</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=H6AWRziR028&pp=0gcJCf8Ao7VqN5tD"> DEF CON 23 - Chris Sistrunk - NSM 101 for ICS</a> </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://attack.mitre.org/matrices/ics/">MITRE ATT&CK for ICS</a></li> </ul> <p> </p>]]></description>

      <title>EP256 Rewiring Democracy &amp; Hacking Trust: Bruce Schneier on the AI Offense-Defense Balance</title>
      <itunes:title>EP256 Rewiring Democracy &amp;amp; Hacking Trust: Bruce Schneier on the AI Offense-Defense Balance</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Mon, 15 Dec 2025 15:11:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[1dc54858-3d03-4da0-8558-7ad4cfdceee6]]></guid>
      <link><![CDATA[https://cloudsecuritypodcast.libsyn.com/ep256-rewiring-democracy-hacking-trust-bruce-schneier-on-the-ai-offense-defense-balance]]></link>
      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.schneier.com/">Bruce Schneier</a></li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Do you believe that AI is going to end up being a net improvement for defenders or attackers?  Is short term vs long term different?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">We're excited about the new book you have coming out with your co-author <a href= "https://mitpress.mit.edu/9780262049948/rewiring-democracy/">Nathan Sanders "Rewiring Democracy"</a>.  We want to ask the same question, but for society: do you think AI is going to end up helping the forces of liberal democracy, or the forces of corruption, illiberalism, and authoritarianism? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">If exploitation is always cheaper than patching (and attackers don't follow as many rules and procedures), do we have a chance here? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">If this requires pervasive and fast "humanless" automatic patching (kinda like what Chrome does for years), will this ever work for most organizations?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Do defenders have to do the same and just discover and fix issues faster? Or can we use AI somehow differently?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Does this make defense in depth more important?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">How do you see AI as changing how society develops and maintains trust? </li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://mitpress.mit.edu/9780262049948/rewiring-democracy/">"Rewiring Democracy"</a> book</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">"<a href= "https://www.amazon.com/Infomocracy-Book-One-Centenal-Cycle/dp/0765392364">Informacracy Trilogy</a>" book</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.schneier.com/blog/archives/2025/10/agentic-ais-ooda-loop-problem.html"> Agentic AI's OODA Loop Problem</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep255-separating-hype-from-hazard-the-truth-about-autonomous-ai-hacking/"> EP255 Separating Hype from Hazard: The Truth About Autonomous AI Hacking</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3737610">AI and Trust</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://spectrum.ieee.org/data-integrity">AI and Data Integrity</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep223-ai-addressable-not-ai-solvable-reflections-from-rsa-2025/"> EP223 AI Addressable, Not AI Solvable: Reflections from RSA 2025</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://medium.com/anton-on-security/rsa-2025-ais-promise-vs-security-s-past-a-reality-check-e06deb3bd579"> RSA 2025: AI's Promise vs. Security's Past — A Reality Check</a></li> </ul> <p> </p>]]></description>

      <title>EP255 Separating Hype from Hazard: The Truth About Autonomous AI Hacking</title>
      <itunes:title>EP255 Separating Hype from Hazard: The Truth About Autonomous AI Hacking</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Mon, 08 Dec 2025 15:11:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[933df24c-b411-4e10-8fe4-7c5bd01ca3f2]]></guid>
      <link><![CDATA[https://cloudsecuritypodcast.libsyn.com/ep255-separating-hype-from-hazard-the-truth-about-autonomous-ai-hacking]]></link>
      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/argvee/">Heather Adkins</a>, VP of Security Engineering, Google</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topic:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">The term "AI Hacking Singularity" sounds like pure sci-fi, yet you and some other very credible folks <a href= "https://www.csoonline.com/article/4069075/autonomous-ai-hacking-and-the-future-of-cybersecurity.html"> are using it to</a> describe an imminent threat. How much of this is hyperbole to shock the complacent, and how much is based on actual, observed capabilities today? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Can autonomous AI agents really achieve that "exploit - at - machine - velocity" without human intervention for the zero-day discovery phase?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">On the other hand, why may it actually not happen?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">When we talk about autonomous AI attack platforms, are we talking about highly resourced nation-states and top-tier criminal groups, or will this capability truly be accessible to the average threat actor within the next 6-12 months? What's the "Metasploit" equivalent for AI-powered exploitation that will be ubiquitous? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Can you paint a realistic picture of the worst-case scenario that autonomous AI hacking enables? Is it a complete breakdown of patch cycles, a global infrastructure collapse, or something worse?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">If attackers are operating at "machine speed," the human defender is fundamentally outmatched. Is there a genuine "AI-to-AI" counter-tactic that doesn't just devolve into an infinite arms race? Or can we counter without AI at all?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Given that AI can expedite vulnerability discovery, how does this amplified threat vector impact the software supply chain? If a dependency is compromised within minutes of a new vulnerability being created, does this force the industry to completely abandon the open-source model, or does it demand a radical, real-time security scanning and patching system that only a handful of tech giants can afford?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Are current proposed regulations, like those focusing on model safety or disclosure, even targeting the right problem? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">If the real danger is the combinatorial speed of autonomous attack agents, what simple, impactful policy change should world governments prioritize right now?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.csoonline.com/article/4069075/autonomous-ai-hacking-and-the-future-of-cybersecurity.html"> "Autonomous AI hacking and the future of cybersecurity"</a> article</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/security-operations-reliability-and-securing-google-with-heather-adkins/"> EP20 Security Operations, Reliability, and Securing Google with Heather Adkins</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://deepmind.google/blog/introducing-codemender-an-ai-agent-for-code-security/"> Introducing CodeMender: an AI agent for code security</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep251-beyond-fancy-scripts-can-ai-red-teaming-find-truly-novel-attacks/"> EP251 Beyond Fancy Scripts: Can AI Red Teaming Find Truly Novel Attacks?</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://danielmiessler.com/">Daniel Miessler site</a> and <a href= "https://podcasts.apple.com/us/podcast/unsupervised-learning/id1099711235"> podcast</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.google.com/blog/transform/how-saif-can-accelerate-secure-ai-experiments-infographic/"> "How SAIF can accelerate secure AI experiments"</a> blog</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://security.googlecloudcommunity.com/ciso-blog-77/staying-on-top-of-ai-developments-4060"> "Staying on top of AI Developments"</a> blog</li> </ul>]]></description>
      
      <content:encoded><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/argvee/">Heather Adkins</a>, VP of Security Engineering, Google</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topic:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">The term "AI Hacking Singularity" sounds like pure sci-fi, yet you and some other very credible folks <a href= "https://www.csoonline.com/article/4069075/autonomous-ai-hacking-and-the-future-of-cybersecurity.html"> are using it to</a> describe an imminent threat. How much of this is hyperbole to shock the complacent, and how much is based on actual, observed capabilities today? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Can autonomous AI agents really achieve that "exploit - at - machine - velocity" without human intervention for the zero-day discovery phase?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">On the other hand, why may it actually not happen?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">When we talk about autonomous AI attack platforms, are we talking about highly resourced nation-states and top-tier criminal groups, or will this capability truly be accessible to the average threat actor within the next 6-12 months? What's the "Metasploit" equivalent for AI-powered exploitation that will be ubiquitous? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Can you paint a realistic picture of the worst-case scenario that autonomous AI hacking enables? Is it a complete breakdown of patch cycles, a global infrastructure collapse, or something worse?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">If attackers are operating at "machine speed," the human defender is fundamentally outmatched. Is there a genuine "AI-to-AI" counter-tactic that doesn't just devolve into an infinite arms race? Or can we counter without AI at all?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Given that AI can expedite vulnerability discovery, how does this amplified threat vector impact the software supply chain? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">If the real danger is the combinatorial speed of autonomous attack agents, what simple, impactful policy change should world governments prioritize right now?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.csoonline.com/article/4069075/autonomous-ai-hacking-and-the-future-of-cybersecurity.html"> "Autonomous AI hacking and the future of cybersecurity"</a> article</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/security-operations-reliability-and-securing-google-with-heather-adkins/"> EP20 Security Operations, Reliability, and Securing Google with Heather Adkins</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://deepmind.google/blog/introducing-codemender-an-ai-agent-for-code-security/"> Introducing CodeMender: an AI agent for code security</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep251-beyond-fancy-scripts-can-ai-red-teaming-find-truly-novel-attacks/"> EP251 Beyond Fancy Scripts: Can AI Red Teaming Find Truly Novel Attacks?</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://danielmiessler.com/">Daniel Miessler site</a> and <a href= "https://podcasts.apple.com/us/podcast/unsupervised-learning/id1099711235"> podcast</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.google.com/blog/transform/how-saif-can-accelerate-secure-ai-experiments-infographic/"> "How SAIF can accelerate secure AI experiments"</a> blog</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://security.googlecloudcommunity.com/ciso-blog-77/staying-on-top-of-ai-developments-4060"> "Staying on top of AI Developments"</a> blog</li> </ul>]]></content:encoded>

    <item>
      <title>EP254 Escaping 1990s Vulnerability Management: From Unauthenticated Scans to AI-Driven Mitigation</title>
      <itunes:title>EP254 Escaping 1990s Vulnerability Management: From Unauthenticated Scans to AI-Driven Mitigation</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Mon, 01 Dec 2025 19:24:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[6e5fe549-400e-4c2d-93b2-1a4d9d3d6c1a]]></guid>
      <link><![CDATA[https://cloudsecuritypodcast.libsyn.com/ep254-escaping-1990s-vulnerability-management-from-unauthenticated-scans-to-ai-driven-mitigation]]></link>
      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/caleb-hoch/">Caleb Hoch</a>, Consulting Manager on Security Transformation Team, Mandiant, Google Cloud</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li>How has vulnerability management (VM) evolved beyond basic scanning and reporting, and what are the biggest gaps between modern practices and what organizations are actually doing?</li> <li>Why are so many organizations stuck with 1990s VM practices?</li> <li>Why mitigation planning is still hard for so many?</li> <li>Why do many organizations, including large ones, still rely on unauthenticated scans despite the known importance of authenticated scanning for accurate results?</li> <li>What constitutes a "gold standard" vulnerability prioritization process in 2025 that moves beyond CVSS scores to incorporate threat intelligence, asset criticality, and other contextual factors?</li> <li>What are the primary human and organizational challenges in vulnerability management, and how can issues like unclear governance, lack of accountability, and fear of system crashes be overcome?</li> <li>How is AI impacting vulnerability management, and does the shift to cloud environments fundamentally change VM practices?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep109-how-google-does-vulnerability-management-the-not-so-secret-secrets/"> EP109 How Google Does Vulnerability Management: The Not So Secret Secrets!</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep246-from-scanners-to-ai-25-years-of-vulnerability-management-with-qualys-ceo-sumedh-thakar/"> EP246 From Scanners to AI: 25 Years of Vulnerability Management with Qualys CEO Sumedh Thakar</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href=       <title>EP252 The Agentic SOC Reality: Governing AI Agents, Data Fidelity, and Measuring Success</title>
      <itunes:title>EP252 The Agentic SOC Reality: Governing AI Agents, Data Fidelity, and Measuring Success</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Mon, 17 Nov 2025 15:11:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[a2ce7639-c182-44db-b583-779b992937e1]]></guid>
      <link><![CDATA[https://cloudsecuritypodcast.libsyn.com/ep252-the-agentic-soc-reality-governing-ai-agents-data-fidelity-and-measuring-success]]></link>
      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guests:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/apabst/">Alexander Pabst</a>, Deputy Group CISO, Allianz</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/lars-k%C3%B6nig-623701102/">Lars Koenig</a>,  Global Head of D&R, Allianz</li> </ul> <p><strong> </strong>Topics:<strong> </strong></p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Moving from traditional SIEM to an agentic SOC model, especially in a heavily regulated insurer, is a massive undertaking. What did the collaboration model with your vendor look like? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Agentic AI introduces a new layer of risk - that of unconstrained or unintended autonomous action. In the context of Allianz, how did you establish the governance framework for the SOC alert triage agents?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Where did you draw the line between fully automated action and the mandatory "human-in-the-loop" for investigation or response?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Agentic triage is only as good as the data it analyzes. From your perspective, what were the biggest challenges - and wins - in ensuring the data fidelity, freshness, and completeness in your SIEM to fuel reliable agent decisions?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">We've been talking about SOC automation for years, but this agentic wave feels different. As a deputy CISO, what was your primary, non-negotiable goal for the agent? Was it purely Mean Time to Respond (MTTR) reduction, or was the bigger strategic prize to fundamentally re-skill and uplevel your Tier 2/3 analysts by removing the low-value alert noise?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">As you built this out, were there any surprises along the way that left you shaking your head or laughing at the unexpected AI behaviors?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">We felt a major lack of proof - Anton <a href= "https://medium.com/anton-on-security/rsa-2025-ais-promise-vs-security-s-past-a-reality-check-e06deb3bd579"> kept asking for pudding</a> - that any of the agentic SOC vendors we saw at RSA had actually achieved anything beyond hype! When it comes to your org, how are you measuring agent success?  What are the key metrics you are using right now?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep238-google-lessons-for-using-ai-agents-for-securing-our-enterprise/"> EP238 Google Lessons for Using AI Agents for Securing Our Enterprise</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep242-the-ai-soc-is-this-the-automation-weve-been-waiting-for/"> EP242 The AI SOC: Is This The Automation We've Been Waiting For?</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep249-data-first-what-really-makes-your-soc-ai-ready/"> EP249 Data First: What Really Makes Your SOC 'AI Ready'?</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep236-accelerated-siem-journey-a-soc-leaders-playbook-for-modernization-and-ai/"> EP236 Accelerated SIEM Journey: A SOC Leader's Playbook for Modernization and AI</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://medium.com/anton-on-security/simple-to-ask-is-your-soc-ai-ready-not-simple-to-answer-858d6789b9fa"> "Simple to Ask: Is Your SOC AI Ready? Not Simple to Answer!" blog</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.google.com/transform/how-google-does-it-building-ai-agents-cybersecurity-defense"> "How Google Does It: Building AI agents for cybersecurity and defense"</a> blog</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Company annual report to look for risk</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.amazon.com/How-Win-Friends-Influence-People/dp/0671027034"> "How to Win Friends and Influence People"</a> by Dale Carnegie</li> <li><a href= "https://www.amazon.com/Will-Make-Boat-Faster-Olympic-Winning/dp/1848769660"> "Will It Make the Boat Go Faster?</a>" book</li> </ul>]]></description>

      <title>EP251 Beyond Fancy Scripts: Can AI Red Teaming Find Truly Novel Attacks?</title>
      <itunes:title>EP251 Beyond Fancy Scripts: Can AI Red Teaming Find Truly Novel Attacks?</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Mon, 10 Nov 2025 15:11:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[654926c6-211d-4b8d-a3a1-38bb9367eb34]]></guid>
      <link><![CDATA[https://cloudsecuritypodcast.libsyn.com/ep251-beyond-fancy-scripts-can-ai-red-teaming-find-truly-novel-attacks]]></link>
      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/adversariel/">Ari Herbert-Voss</a>, CEO at <a href="https://www.runsybil.com/">RunSybil</a></li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">The market already has Breach and Attack Simulation (BAS), for testing known TTPs. You're calling this 'AI-powered' red teaming. Is this just a fancy LLM stringing together known attacks, or is there a genuine agent here that can discover a truly novel attack path that a human hasn't scripted for it?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Let's talk about the 'so what?' problem. Pentest reports are famous for becoming shelf-ware. How do you turn a complex AI finding into an actionable ticket for a developer, and more importantly, how do you help a CISO decide which of the thousand 'criticals' to actually fix first?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You're asking customers to unleash a 'hacker AI' in their production environment. That's terrifying. What are the 'do no harm' guardrails? How do you guarantee your AI won't accidentally rm -rf a critical server or cause a denial of service while it's 'exploring'?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You mentioned the AI is particularly good at finding authentication bugs. Why that specific category? What's the secret sauce there, and what's the reaction from customers when you show them those types of flaws?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Is this AI meant to replace a human red teamer, or make them better? Does it automate the boring stuff so experts can focus on creative business logic attacks, or is the ultimate goal to automate the entire red team function away?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">So, is this just about finding holes, or are you closing the loop for the blue team? Can the attack paths your AI finds be automatically translated into high-fidelity detection rules? Is the end goal a continuous purple team engine that's constantly training our defenses?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Also, what about fixing? What makes your findings more fixable?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What will happen to red team testing in 2-3 years if this technology gets better?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resource:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.zetter-zeroday.com/about/">Kim Zetter Zero Day blog</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep230-ai-red-teaming-surprises-strategies-and-lessons-from-google/"> EP230 AI Red Teaming: Surprises, Strategies, and Lessons from Google</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep217-red-teaming-ai-uncovering-surprises-facing-new-threats-and-the-same-old-mistakes/"> EP217 Red Teaming AI: Uncovering Surprises, Facing New Threats, and the Same Old Mistakes?</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep68-how-we-attack-ai-learn-more-at-our-rsa-panel/"> EP68 How We Attack AI? Learn More at Our RSA Panel!</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep71-attacking-google-to-defend-google-how-google-does-red-team/"> EP71 Attacking Google to Defend Google: How Google Does Red Team</a></li> </ul> <p> </p>]]></description>
      
      <content:encoded><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/adversariel/">Ari Herbert-Voss</a>, CEO at <a href="https://www.runsybil.com/">RunSybil</a></li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">The market already has Breach and Attack Simulation (BAS), for testing known TTPs. You're calling this 'AI-powered' red teaming. Is this just a fancy LLM stringing together known attacks, or is there a genuine agent here that can discover a truly novel attack path that a human hasn't scripted for it?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Let's talk about the 'so what?' problem. Pentest reports are famous for becoming shelf-ware. How do you turn a complex AI finding into an actionable ticket for a developer, and more importantly, how do you help a CISO decide which of the thousand 'criticals' to actually fix first?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You're asking customers to unleash a 'hacker AI' in their production environment. That's terrifying. What are the 'do no harm' guardrails? How do you guarantee your AI won't accidentally rm -rf a critical server or cause a denial of service while it's 'exploring'?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You mentioned the AI is particularly good at finding authentication bugs. Why that specific category? What's the secret sauce there, and what's the reaction from customers when you show them those types of flaws?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Is this AI meant to replace a human red teamer, or make them better? Does it automate the boring stuff so experts can focus on creative business logic attacks, or is the ultimate goal to automate the entire red team function away?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">So, is this just about finding holes, or are you closing the loop for the blue team? Can the attack paths your AI finds be automatically translated into high-fidelity detection rules? Is the end goal a continuous purple team engine that's constantly training our defenses?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Also, what about fixing? What makes your findings more fixable?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What will happen to red team testing in 2-3 years if this technology gets better?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resource:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.zetter-zeroday.com/about/">Kim Zetter Zero Day blog</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep230-ai-red-teaming-surprises-strategies-and-lessons-from-google/"> EP230 AI Red Teaming: Surprises, Strategies, and Lessons from Google</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep217-red-teaming-ai-uncovering-surprises-facing-new-threats-and-the-same-old-mistakes/"> EP217 Red Teaming AI: Uncovering Surprises, Facing New Threats, and the Same Old Mistakes?</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep68-how-we-attack-ai-learn-more-at-our-rsa-panel/"> EP68 How We Attack AI? Learn More at Our RSA Panel!</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep71-attacking-google-to-defend-google-how-google-does-red-team/"> EP71 Attacking Google to Defend Google: How Google Does Red Team</a></li> </ul> <p> </p>]]></content:encoded>

      <title>EP250 The End of "Collect Everything"? Moving from Centralization to Data Access?</title>
      <itunes:title>EP250 The End of &quot;Collect Everything&quot;? Moving from Centralization to Data Access?</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Mon, 03 Nov 2025 12:11:00 +0000</pubDate>

      <title>EP249 Data First: What Really Makes Your SOC 'AI Ready'?</title>
      <itunes:title>EP249 Data First: What Really Makes Your SOC &#39;AI Ready&#39;?</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Mon, 27 Oct 2025 14:11:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[b0552125-809d-4a62-b041-12569bdbf14f]]></guid>
      <link><![CDATA[https://cloudsecuritypodcast.libsyn.com/ep249-data-first-what-really-makes-your-soc-ai-ready]]></link>
      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/monzymerza/">Monzy Merza</a>, co-founder and CEO at <a href= "https://www.crogl.com/">Crogl</a></li> </ul> <p>Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">We often hear about the aspirational idea of an "IronMan suit" for the SOC—a system that empowers analysts to be faster and more effective. What does this ideal future of security operations look like from your perspective, and what are the primary obstacles preventing SOCs from achieving it today?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You've also raised a metaphor of AI in the SOC as a "Dr. Jekyll and Mr. Hyde" situation. Could you walk us through what you see as the "Jekyll"—the noble, beneficial promise of AI—and what are the factors that can turn it into the dangerous "Mr. Hyde"?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Let's drill down into the heart of the "Mr. Hyde" problem: the data. Many believe that AI can fix a team's messy data, but you've noted that "it's all about the data, duh." What's the story?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">"AI ready SOC" - What is the foundational work a SOC needs to do to ensure their data is AI-ready, and what happens when they skip this step?  </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">And is there anything we can do to use AI to help with this foundational problem?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">How do we measure progress towards AI SOC? What gets better at what time? How would we know? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What SOC metrics will show improvement? Will anything get worse? </li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep242-the-ai-soc-is-this-the-automation-weve-been-waiting-for/"> EP242 The AI SOC: Is This The Automation We've Been Waiting For?</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep170-redefining-security-operations-practical-applications-of-genai-in-the-soc/"> EP170 Redefining Security Operations: Practical Applications of GenAI in the SOC</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep227-ai-native-mdr-betting-on-the-future-of-security-operations/"> EP227 AI-Native MDR: Betting on the Future of Security Operations?</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep236-accelerated-siem-journey-a-soc-leaders-playbook-for-modernization-and-ai/"> EP236 Accelerated SIEM Journey: A SOC Leader's Playbook for Modernization and AI</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep238-google-lessons-for-using-ai-agents-for-securing-our-enterprise/"> EP238 Google Lessons for Using AI Agents for Securing Our Enterprise</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://medium.com/anton-on-security/simple-to-ask-is-your-soc-ai-ready-not-simple-to-answer-858d6789b9fa"> "Simple to Ask: Is Your SOC AI Ready?       <title>EP248 Cloud IR Tabletop Wins: How to Stop Playing Security Theater and Start Practicing</title>
      <itunes:title>EP248 Cloud IR Tabletop Wins: How to Stop Playing Security Theater and Start Practicing</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Mon, 20 Oct 2025 14:11:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[d53227e7-0984-46ca-be43-022b6a3e912c]]></guid>
      <link><![CDATA[https://cloudsecuritypodcast.libsyn.com/ep248-cloud-ir-tabletop-wins-how-to-stop-playing-security-theater-and-start-practicing]]></link>
      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/jibranilyas/">Jibran Ilyas</a>, Director for Incident Response at Google Cloud</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What is this tabletop thing, please tell us about running a good security incident tabletop? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Why are tabletops for incident response preparedness so amazingly effective yet rarely done well?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">This is cheap/easy/useful so why do so many fail to do it? But there is little ransomware in the cloud, no?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What are other good cloud tabletop scenarios?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep60-impersonating-service-accounts-in-gcp-and-beyond-cloud-security-is-about-iam/"> EP60 Impersonating Service Accounts in GCP and Beyond: Cloud Security Is About IAM?</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep179-teamwork-under-stress-expedition-behavior-in-cybersecurity-incident-response/"> EP179 Teamwork Under Stress: Expedition Behavior in Cybersecurity Incident Response</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep222-from-post-ir-lessons-to-proactive-security-deconstructing-mandiant-m-trends/"> EP222 From Post-IR Lessons to Proactive Security: Deconstructing Mandiant M-Trends</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep177-cloud-incident-confessions-top-5-mistakes-leading-to-breaches-from-mandiant/"> EP177 Cloud Incident Confessions: Top 5 Mistakes Leading to Breaches from Mandiant</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep158-ghostbusters-for-the-cloud-who-you-gonna-call-for-cloud-forensics/"> EP158 Ghostbusters for the Cloud: Who You Gonna Call for Cloud Forensics</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep98-how-to-cloud-ir-or-why-attackers-become-cloud-native-faster/"> EP98 How to Cloud IR or Why Attackers Become Cloud Native Faster?</a></li> </ul> <p> </p>]]></description>

      <title>EP245 From Consumer Chatbots to Enterprise Guardrails: Securing Real AI Adoption</title>
      <itunes:title>EP245 From Consumer Chatbots to Enterprise Guardrails: Securing Real AI Adoption</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Mon, 29 Sep 2025 14:11:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[b89b1606-1e5d-4810-b15a-1c44f11dc723]]></guid>
      <link><![CDATA[https://cloudsecuritypodcast.libsyn.com/ep245-from-consumer-chatbots-to-enterprise-guardrails-securing-real-ai-adoption]]></link>
      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/rcaccia/">Rick Caccia</a>, CEO and Co-Founder, <a href="https://witness.ai/">Witness AI</a></li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">In what ways is the current wave of enterprise AI adoption different from previous technology shifts? If we say "but it is different this time", then why?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What is your take on "consumer grade AI for business" vs enterprise AI?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">A lot of this sounds a bit like the CASB era circa 2014. How is this different with AI? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">The concept of "routing prompts for risk and cost management" is intriguing. Here's a more secure way forward</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://security.googlecloudcommunity.com/community-blog-42/shadow-ai-strikes-back-enterprise-ai-absent-oversight-in-the-age-of-gen-ai-3963"> Shadow AI Strikes Back: Enterprise AI Absent Oversight in the Age of Gen AI</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.google.com/blog/products/identity-security/cloud-ciso-perspectives-how-google-secures-ai-agents/?e=48754805"> Cloud CISO Perspectives: How Google secures AI Agents</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.amazon.com/Soul-New-Machine-Tracy-Kidder/dp/0316491977"> "The Soul of a New Machine"</a> book</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://arxiv.org/html/2411.01077v1">Emoji Attack: A Method for Misleading Judge LLMs in Safety Risk Detection</a></li> </ul> <p> </p>]]></description>
      
      <content:encoded><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/rcaccia/">Rick Caccia</a>, CEO and Co-Founder, <a href="https://witness.ai/">Witness AI</a></li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">In what ways is the current wave of enterprise AI adoption different from previous technology shifts?       <title>EP244 The Future of SOAPA: Jon Oltsik on Platform Consolidation vs. Best-of-Breed in the Age of Agentic AI</title>
      <itunes:title>EP244 The Future of SOAPA: Jon Oltsik on Platform Consolidation vs. Best-of-Breed in the Age of Agentic AI</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Mon, 22 Sep 2025 14:11:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[2b177495-9eac-4c92-8536-89b31ff21edd]]></guid>
      <link><![CDATA[https://cloudsecuritypodcast.libsyn.com/ep244-the-future-of-soapa-jon-oltsik-on-platform-consolidation-vs-best-of-breed-in-the-age-of-agentic-ai]]></link>
      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/jonoltsik/">Jon Oltsik</a>, security researcher, ex-ESG analyst</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You invented the concept of <a href= "https://www.csoonline.com/article/567745/soapa-vs-soar-how-these-security-terms-differ.html"> SOAPA</a> – Security Operations & Analytics Platform Architecture. As we look towards SOAPA 2025, how do you see the ongoing debate between consolidating security around a single platform versus a more disaggregated, best-of-breed approach playing out? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What are the key drivers for either strategy in today's complex environments? How can we have both "<a href= "https://medium.com/anton-on-security/decoupled-siem-brilliant-or-stupid-ada2c3142b2b">decoupling</a>" and platformization going at the same time?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">With all the buzz around Generative AI and Agentic AI, how do you envision these technologies changing the future of the Security Operations Center (and SOAPA of course)? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Where do you see AI really work today in the SOC and what is the proof of that actually happening? What does a realistic "AI SOC" look like in the next few years, and what are the practical implications for security teams?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">"Integration" is always a hot topic in security - and it has been for decades. Within the context of SOAPA and the adoption of advanced analytics, where do you see the most critical integration challenges today – whether it's vendor-centric ecosystems, strategic partnerships, or the push for open standards?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Jon Oltsik <a href= "https://www.thecube.net/events/program/cybersecurity-bridge">"The Cybersecurity Bridge"</a> podcast (<a href= "https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=GlJaquldlhE&list=PLenh213llmcahJrPzS8jdF-sunb9VRBpl">Anton on it</a>)</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep236-accelerated-siem-journey-a-soc-leaders-playbook-for-modernization-and-ai/"> EP236 Accelerated SIEM Journey: A SOC Leader's Playbook for Modernization and AI</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep242-the-ai-soc-is-this-the-automation-weve-been-waiting-for/"> EP242 The AI SOC: Is This The Automation We've Been Waiting For?</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep202-beyond-tiered-socs-detection-as-code-and-the-rise-of-response-engineering/"> EP202 Beyond Tiered SOCs: Detection as Code and the Rise of Response Engineering</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep180-soc-crossroads-optimization-vs-transformation-two-paths-for-security-operations-center/"> EP180 SOC Crossroads: Optimization vs Transformation -       <title>EP242 The AI SOC: Is This The Automation We've Been Waiting For?</title>
      <itunes:title>EP242 The AI SOC: Is This The Automation We&#39;ve Been Waiting For?</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Mon, 08 Sep 2025 14:11:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[a1524b58-ea21-44ed-ab8f-e08f4d7d0b3f]]></guid>
      <link><![CDATA[https://cloudsecuritypodcast.libsyn.com/ep242-the-ai-soc-is-this-the-automation-weve-been-waiting-for]]></link>
      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" aria-level="1"> <p dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/apbarros/">Augusto Barros</a>, Principal Product Manager, <a href= "https://www.prophetsecurity.ai/">Prophet Security</a>, <a href= "https://www.gartner.com/analyst/bdcc02ba73">ex-Gartner analyst</a></p> </li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What is your definition of "AI SOC"?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What will AI change in a SOC? What will the post-AI SOC look like? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What are the primary mechanisms by which AI SOC tools reduce attacker dwell time, and what challenges do they face in maintaining signal fidelity?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Why would this wave of SOC automation (namely, AI SOC)  work now, if it did not fully succeed before (SOAR)?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">How do we measure progress towards AI SOC? What gets better at what time? How would we know? What SOC metrics will show improvement?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What common misconceptions or challenges have organizations encountered during the initial stages of AI SOC adoption, and how can they be overcome?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Do you have a timeline for SOC AI adoption? What's after that?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://hubs.ly/Q03Hq--x0">"State of AI in Security Operations 2025"</a> report</li> <li role="presentation"><a href= "https://medium.com/anton-on-security/a-brief-guide-for-dealing-with-humanless-soc-idiots-3c2f1a5b26e9"> A Brief Guide for Dealing with 'Humanless SOC' Idiots</a></li> </ul> <p> </p>]]></description>
      
      <content:encoded><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" aria-level="1"> <p dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/apbarros/">Augusto Barros</a>, Principal Product Manager, <a href= "https://www.prophetsecurity.ai/">Prophet Security</a>, <a href= "https://www.gartner.com/analyst/bdcc02ba73">ex-Gartner analyst</a></p> </li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What is your definition of "AI SOC"?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What will AI change in a SOC? What will the post-AI SOC look like? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What are the primary mechanisms by which AI SOC tools reduce attacker dwell time, and what challenges do they face in maintaining signal fidelity?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Why would this wave of SOC automation (namely, AI SOC) work now, if it did not fully succeed before (SOAR)?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">How do we measure progress towards AI SOC? What gets better at what time? How would we know? What SOC metrics will show improvement?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What common misconceptions or challenges have organizations encountered during the initial stages of AI SOC adoption, and how can they be overcome?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Do you have a timeline for SOC AI adoption? Sure, everybody wants AI alerts triage? What's next? What's after that?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://hubs.ly/Q03Hq--x0">"State of AI in Security Operations 2025"</a> report</li> <li       <title>EP241 From Black Box to Building Blocks:  More Modern Detection Engineering Lessons from Google</title>
      <itunes:title>EP241 From Black Box to Building Blocks:  More Modern Detection Engineering Lessons from Google</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Mon, 01 Sep 2025 14:11:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[e09b2a35-a65c-48fb-9e12-6a5a5a437aca]]></guid>
      <link><![CDATA[https://cloudsecuritypodcast.libsyn.com/ep241-from-black-box-to-building-blocks-more-modern-detection-engineering-lessons-from-google]]></link>
      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "mailto:rickcorrea@google.com"><span data-rich-links= "{"per_n":"Rick Correa","per_e":"rickcorrea@google.com","type":"person"}"> Rick Correa</span></a>,Uber TL Google SecOps, Google Cloud</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">On the 3rd anniversary of Curated Detections, you've grown from 70 rules to over 4700. Can you walk us through that journey? What were some of the key inflection points and what have been the biggest lessons learned in scaling a detection portfolio so massively?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Historically the <a href= "https://cloud.google.com/chronicle/docs/detection/curated-detections"> SecOps Curated Detection</a> content was opaque, which led to, understandably, a bit of customer friction. We've recently made nearly all of that content transparent and editable by users. What were the challenges in that transition?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You make a distinction between "Detection-as-Code" and a more mature "Software Engineering" paradigm. What gets better for a security team when they move beyond just version control and a CI/CD pipeline and start incorporating things like unit testing, readability reviews, and performance testing for their detections?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">The idea of a "Goldilocks Zone" for detections is intriguing – not too many, not too few. How do you find that balance, and what are the metrics that matter when measuring the effectiveness of a detection program? You mentioned customer feedback is important, but a <a href= "https://medium.com/@tam.tamanna18/understanding-the-confusion-matrix-and-its-applications-3a1c33d799af"> confusion matrix</a> isn't possible, why is that?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You talk about enabling customers to use your "building blocks" to create their own detections. Can you give us a practical example of how a customer might use a building block for something like detecting VPN and Tor traffic to augment their security?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You have started using LLMs for reviewing the explainability of human-generated metadata. Can you expand on that? What have you found are the ripe areas for AI in detection engineering, and can you share any anecdotes of where AI has succeeded and where it has failed?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr"><br />    Resources</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep197-siem-decoupled-or-not-and-security-data-lakes-a-google-secops-perspective/"> EP197 SIEM (Decoupled or Not), and Security Data Lakes: A Google SecOps Perspective</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep231-beyond-the-buzzword-practical-detection-as-code-in-the-enterprise/"> EP231 Beyond the Buzzword: Practical Detection as Code in the Enterprise</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep181-detection-engineering-deep-dive-from-career-paths-to-scaling-soc-teams/"> EP181 Detection Engineering Deep Dive: From Career Paths to Scaling SOC Teams</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep139-what-is-chronicle-beyond-xdr-and-into-the-next-generation-of-security-operations/"> EP139 What is Chronicle? Beyond XDR and into the Next Generation of Security Operations</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep123-the-good-the-bad-and-the-epic-of-threat-detection-at-scale-with-panther/"> EP123 The Good, the Bad, and the Epic of Threat Detection at Scale with Panther</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://medium.com/anton-on-security/back-to-cooking-detection-engineer-vs-detection-consumer-again-ac41b006e8e3"> "Back to Cooking: Detection Engineer vs Detection Consumer, Again?"</a> blog</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://medium.com/anton-on-security/on-trust-and-transparency-in-detection-52ae6a29afdf"> "On Trust and Transparency in Detection"</a> blog</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/company/detection-engineering-weekly/posts/?feedView=all"> "Detection Engineering Weekly"</a> newsletter</li> <li><a href= "https://www.amazon.com/Practical-Threat-Detection-Engineering-hands/dp/1801076715"> "Practical Threat Detection Engineering"</a> book</li> </ul>]]></description>

      <title>EP240 Cyber Resiliency for the Rest of Us: Making it Happen on a Real-World Budget</title>

      <pubDate>Mon, 25 Aug 2025 14:11:00 +0000</pubDate>

      <title>EP239 Linux Security: The Detection and Response Disconnect and Where Is My Agentless EDR</title>
      <itunes:title>EP239 Linux Security: The Detection and Response Disconnect and Where Is My Agentless EDR</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Mon, 18 Aug 2025 14:11:00 +0000</pubDate>
      <guid isPermaLink="false"><![CDATA[0d2ffdcc-d3c3-442e-809d-5c089e40a01d]]></guid>
      <link><![CDATA[https://cloudsecuritypodcast.libsyn.com/ep239-linux-security-the-detection-and-response-disconnect-and-where-is-my-agentless-edr]]></link>
      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/craighrowland/">Craig H. Rowland</a>, Founder and CEO, <a href="https://sandflysecurity.com/">Sandfly Security</a></li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Topics:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">When it comes to Linux environments – spanning on-prem, cloud, and even–gasp–hybrid setups – where are you seeing the most significant blind spots for security teams today? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">There's sometimes a perception that Linux is inherently more secure or less of a malware target than Windows. Could you break down some of the fundamental differences in how malware behaves on Linux versus Windows, and why that matters for defenders in the cloud?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">'Living off the Land' isn't a new concept, but on Linux, it feels like attackers have a particularly rich set of native tools at their disposal. What are some of the more subtly abused but legitimate Linux utilities you're seeing weaponized in cloud attacks, and how does that complicate detection?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">When you weigh agent-based versus agentless monitoring in cloud and containerized Linux environments, what are the operational trade-offs and outcome trade-offs security teams really need to consider? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">SSH keys are the de facto keys to the kingdom in many Linux environments. Beyond just 'use strong passphrases,' what are the critical, often overlooked, risks associated with SSH key management, credential theft, and subsequent lateral movement that you see plaguing organizations, especially at scale in the cloud?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">What are the biggest operational hurdles teams face when trying to conduct incident response effectively and rapidly across such a distributed Linux environment, and what's key to overcoming them?</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Resources:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep194-deep-dive-into-adr-application-detection-and-response/"> EP194 Deep Dive into ADR - Application Detection and Response</a></li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://cloud.withgoogle.com/cloudsecurity/podcast/ep228-siem-in-2025-still-hard-reimagining-detection-at-cloud-scale-and-with-more-pipelines/"> EP228 SIEM in 2025: Still Hard? Reimagining Detection at Cloud Scale and with More Pipelines</a></li> </ul> <p> </p>]]></description>
      
      <content:encoded><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/craighrowland/">Craig H.       <title>EP237 Making Security Personal at the Speed and Scale of TikTok</title>
      <itunes:title>EP237 Making Security Personal at the Speed and Scale of TikTok</itunes:title>
      <pubDate>Mon, 04 Aug 2025 14:11:00 +0000</pubDate>

      <description><![CDATA[<p dir="ltr">Guest:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation"><a href= "https://www.linkedin.com/in/kimalbarella/">Kim Albarella</a>, Global Head of Security, TikTok</li> </ul> <p dir="ltr">Questions:</p> <ul> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Security is part of your DNA. In your day to day at TikTok, what are some tips you'd share with users about staying safe online?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">Many regulations were written with older technologies in mind. How do you bridge the gap between these legacy requirements and the realities of a modern, microservices-based tech stack like TikTok's, ensuring both compliance and agility?</li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">You have a background in compliance and risk management. How do you approach demonstrating the effectiveness of security controls, not just their existence, especially given the rapid pace of change in both technology and regulations? </li> <li dir="ltr" role="presentation">TikTok operates on a global scale, facing a complex web of varying regulations and user expectations.
